## [SLED: Sensor Slicing for Per-Sensor Isolation in O-RAN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21937) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Sensor Slicing (SL), a MAC-level isolation mechanism that provides deterministic isolation for individual sensor streams within a single user equipment (UE) in O-RAN. Using agentic coding, SL was implemented in two O-RAN-compliant packages, reducing development time by 400 times (2 h 40 min vs. 6.5 weeks) and requiring 228-fold fewer human prompts. This work addresses the contention problem in modern sensing systems where bulk data transfers can starve latency-critical telemetry, and demonstrates that agentic coding can drastically accelerate the development of complex network protocols. The SLED framework enables per-sensor radio and network isolation across edge, campus, and remote HPC tiers, which is crucial for emerging AI/SI inference and training applications. Over-the-air experiments show that conventional network slicing causes critical delays to increase more than 2,300-fold under high traffic load, while SLED maintains stable latency. Scaling evaluations on two RAN stacks show throughput remains within 0.9% and 1.4% of expected allocations as the number of sensor slices increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: O-RAN (Open Radio Access Network) is an architecture that disaggregates RAN components and uses open interfaces to improve flexibility and interoperability. Network slicing allows multiple virtual networks to be created on a shared physical infrastructure, but traditional slicing operates at the device level, not per sensor stream. Agentic coding is an AI-driven approach where an AI agent plans, writes, tests, and modifies code with minimal human intervention.

## [Commitment-Verdict Gap Explains When Reasoning Models Should Stop Thinking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21946) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a commitment-verdict gap to explain when reasoning models should stop thinking, showing that final-correctness probes alone are insufficient and that stopping can improve accuracy. On MATH-500, at all 379 points where DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B holds a correct answer, continuing to think loses 3.6 points on average (95% CI [2.2, 5.2]). This work provides a theoretical framework and empirical evidence that overthinking can harm accuracy, challenging the assumption that longer reasoning always helps. It offers actionable insight for designing stopping criteria in reasoning models, which could lead to more efficient and accurate inference. The commitment-verdict gap equals the expected remaining change in answer quality and the accuracy gained by stopping. An oracle stopping rule gains at most +1.8 and +2.5 points at 1.5B and 7B model scales, and declining accuracy curves come from which chains remain rather than from chains still reasoning getting worse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Reasoning models like DeepSeek-R1 generate long chains of thought before producing a final answer. A common assumption is that more thinking improves accuracy, but recent research shows models can reach a correct answer early and then lose it by continuing. Final-correctness probes are linear classifiers trained on internal activations to predict whether the final answer will be correct, but they only estimate the verdict, not the commitment.

## [Public Comments in AI Regulation: Business Groups Cite Less Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21948) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper analyzes 2,664 public comments on 40 potential AI regulations in the USA, finding that business commenters introduce less evidence overall, while government, university, and civil society commenters provide significantly more research and legal sources. This empirical study challenges the assumption that public comments inherently enable regulatory capture by industry, showing instead that different stakeholder types play distinct roles in supplying evidence, which has implications for AI governance and how regulators access information. The study computationally extracts formal citations and informal references from comments, and finds that self-citation is prevalent across all groups but a small cluster of organizations accounts for over half of all self-citations; the publication status of cited research is nearly evenly split between preprints and peer-reviewed articles.

## [WebAgents Unbundles Agent Creation, Hosting, and Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21978) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WebAgents introduces a signed agent.pkg that carries portable workflows, skills, schemas, and resource requirements, and an OpenClaw-based agent-native browser/runtime that verifies the package, obtains authorization, and binds it at run time to user-controlled models, tools, permissions, and budgets. The authors demonstrate three unrelated workflows executed unchanged under local and API-backed models, and in a controlled suite the system blocks all 716 injected contract violations while accepting all 295 matched benign controls. This architecture decouples agent publication from execution environments, potentially enabling an interoperable ecosystem where agents can move across creators, hosts, and runtimes without transferring authority over user resources. It addresses a key tension in the emerging Agentic Web between portability and security, which could influence how future agent platforms handle permissions and trust. The system uses an OpenClaw-based browser/runtime that verifies signed packages and enforces user-defined permissions and budgets, with median automated pre-execution processing below 0.5 seconds for all three packages. The authors outline a path toward NANDA-based discovery and interoperable package ecosystems while retaining execution authority in the user's runtime.

## [Formal Counterfactual Explanations Bridged with Abductive and Contrastive XAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22010) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper characterizes closest-world counterfactual explanations as a disjunctive normal form (DNF) over all minimally distant counterexample instances and introduces conjunctive counterfactual explanations (CFXps), a subclass restricted to conjunctions of feature literals that guarantee prediction change. It establishes structural results linking contrastive explanations (CXps), abductive explanations (AXps), and CFXps, showing that every CXp induces an AXp for its witness instance and that every CXp together with a witness AXp induces a CFXp whose support is determined by that AXp. This work bridges the philosophical closest-world semantics of counterfactuals with the formal XAI framework that relies on efficiently computable abductive and contrastive explanations, potentially enabling more rigorous and modular computation of counterfactual explanations. It could benefit researchers in explainable AI by providing a theoretical foundation for generating counterfactuals that guarantee prediction change beyond a single instance. The theoretical results yield a modular approach for computing CFXps using existing AXp/CXp machinery, and experiments on benchmarks with tree ensembles and neural networks show that CFXp extraction is practically efficient. Compared with popular point-based counterfactual explainers, CFXps generally require no more feature changes and can guarantee the counterfactual prediction beyond a single instance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations answer 'what if' questions by identifying minimal changes to input features that would alter a model's prediction, often interpreted under closest-world semantics from philosophy. Formal XAI provides feature-based abductive explanations (AXps) that give sufficient conditions for a prediction and contrastive explanations (CXps) that give necessary conditions, with efficient computation via minimal hitting-set duality. Disjunctive normal form (DNF) is a standardized way to represent Boolean expressions as a disjunction of conjunctions, useful for characterizing sets of counterexamples.

## [SkillBuffBench: A Paired Benchmark for Agent Skills in Realistic Deployment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22017) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillBuffBench introduces a paired evaluation protocol that treats Skill access as a three-level factor (no Skill, a curated Skill, and automatic invocation from an installed library), crossed with memory on/off, four agent harnesses, and multiple frontier models from Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, and Chinese open-weight providers. The paper describes the benchmark design, evaluation protocol, and current expert-contributed tasks in materials science and biology, with results to be added once runs are complete. This benchmark addresses a real gap: most evidence for Agent Skills comes from curated, blank-slate settings, not from deployment where agents choose from an installed library and carry memory. By systematically crossing skill access modes, memory, harnesses, and models, it will provide the agent/LLM tooling community with empirical data on how much Skills actually help in realistic conditions. Every run is marked by a deterministic verifier, uncertainty is reported with a paired task-level bootstrap, and cost, agent rounds, and wall-clock time are reported alongside the score rather than folded into it. The task suite is currently in materials science and biology and will be extended on a rolling basis, with evaluation still in progress.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Agent Skills are file-based packages of procedural knowledge that a coding agent can discover and load on demand, now shipped by every major agent harness. An agent harness is the software infrastructure around an LLM that turns it into an agent, handling tool use, context management, and execution loops. Prior evaluations typically hand-pick the right Skill for each task and start the agent from a blank slate, which does not reflect deployment where agents select from whatever library is installed and often retain memory from earlier work.

## [More Reasoning Effort Boosts LLM Shopping Agents' Confidence, Not Correctness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22033) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper benchmarks ten frontier LLMs on three shopping-agent tasks built from WebShop, Amazon's SQID, and Wayfair's WANDS, finding that increasing reasoning effort does not statistically improve purchase decision quality but does significantly raise stated confidence. This counterintuitive finding challenges the assumption that more test-time compute translates to better agentic decisions, suggesting that current reasoning scaling may inflate confidence without improving real-world task performance and that evaluation should track calibration, not just accuracy. The study also measures calibration and finds most models are poorly calibrated at every effort level, and it reveals strongly shared errors: when one model errs on a task, several others tend to err on the same task, often picking the same wrong product.

**Background**: LLM shopping agents are autonomous systems that process multimodal product listings (images and text) and recommend purchases on behalf of consumers. WebShop is a simulated e-commerce environment for evaluating language agents on real-world shopping tasks, while Amazon's SQID and Wayfair's WANDS are product search relevance datasets used to build product-choice benchmarks. Reasoning effort refers to the amount of test-time computation a model is allowed to spend before answering.

## [MedGuardBench: A Patient-Oriented Benchmark for Personalized Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22069) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MedGuardBench, a drug-disjoint benchmark of 5,001 scenarios across 87 medications for patient-conditioned medication guarding, which predicts a safety verdict and 17 risk categories from a patient profile and prescription. They also developed personalized reasoning guardrails with Personalized Reasoning Distillation, Context Unrolling, and a rule-based reward (guided GRPO) to improve safety verdicts. Current guardrails judge content without considering who is affected, but safety is personal—the same action can be safe for one person and unsafe for another. This work addresses an important gap in safety evaluation for healthcare AI, where personalized risk assessment is critical for patient safety. Standard guardrails rely on surface cues like wording and profile presence rather than patient facts. Personalized Reasoning Distillation links risks to relevant patient fields, Context Unrolling makes models check each field themselves (reducing false alarms on most backbones), and guided GRPO improves unsafe recall and complex-risk detection on some backbones but increases false alarms on others; profile removal and swapping confirm gains come from patient information, not fine-tuning alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: AI guardrails are safety mechanisms that monitor and filter AI outputs to prevent harm, and in healthcare they are crucial because decisions can directly affect patient care. Personalized reasoning aims to tailor AI responses to individual user contexts, while context unrolling is a technique where models iteratively build and incorporate context during inference. MedGuardBench evaluates whether guardrails can use patient-specific information to make accurate safety judgments about medication prescriptions.

## [Reversibility Classes: A Five-Level Vocabulary for AI Agent Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Reversibility Classes, a declared five-level vocabulary with argument-sensitive rules and conservative defaults, and evaluates a session guard that gates irreversible actions. The implementation covers 536 tools across 20 MCP servers, with all 738 rule-case checks conforming to expectations and the guard catching all 29 attributable attack-positive runs versus 18 for a read/write proxy. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent safety: permission to call a tool does not indicate whether a mistake can be undone. By making recovery assumptions explicit and testable, it offers a practical framework for safer agent deployment, especially as tool-using agents increasingly modify files, databases, and public information. The evaluation includes an AI-assisted reviewer who approved 148 ratings (75 matching the registry), which the authors explicitly note is not independent agreement. Controlled experiments across file, Git, SQLite, and three-browser scenarios show recovery depends on captured state, later changes, editor storage, and recovery route, and expose a Git merge-state exception; the paper does not establish registry-wide accuracy or reduced production harm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents can change files, databases, access rights, and public information, but existing permission systems only control whether an action is allowed, not whether it can be undone. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard for connecting AI applications to external tools and data sources, and the paper's registry spans 20 MCP servers. Reversibility Classes aim to classify actions by how easily they can be rolled back, helping developers add safeguards like human approval gates for irreversible operations.

## [EvoDisco: Evolutionary Strategy Discovery for LLM Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22102) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoDisco formulates agent memory evolution as an evolutionary strategy discovery problem, where a learnable policy selects and instantiates one of four semantic operators (Abstract, Refine, Bridge, Reframe) to transform memory dynamically. It is updated with a bi-level fitness signal and outperforms baselines like MemP and MemRL on ALFWorld, WebShop, and LifelongAgentBench. This work moves beyond static memory update rules by letting agents learn when and how to transform their memory, which could lead to more adaptive and efficient LLM agents in long-horizon tasks. The significant gains on ALFWorld suggest potential for improving agent performance in interactive environments. The policy is trained with a bi-level fitness: an inner signal scores each transformation by retrieval-quality improvement regularized by memory complexity, while an outer signal evaluates strategic efficacy over past decisions using regret, prediction error, and diversity. Ablations confirm both fitness levels and the operator library contribute to the gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents accumulate experience in memory, but most methods use fixed operators and schedules to update it. EvoDisco treats memory evolution as a strategy discovery problem, where the agent learns to select and instantiate semantic operators (Abstract, Refine, Bridge, Reframe) based on current memory state and task history. This is inspired by evolutionary strategies and bi-level optimization in reinforcement learning.

## [MathEye uses angelic proof checking to fix LLM-generated Lean proofs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22103) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MathEye introduces angelic proof checking, an optimistic execution model that replaces failing proof fragments with placeholders to isolate unresolved obligations, then uses MaxSAT optimization over a proof dependence graph to select a minimal set of essential obligations for human resolution. Experiments on 30 benchmarks show it solves 93.3% of problems while reducing user interactions by 55%. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in LLM-assisted formal theorem proving: imperfect proof scripts that fail on complex theorems. By minimizing human intervention, it could make formal verification more practical for mathematicians and significantly improve Lean proof completion rates. The system formulates obligation selection as a MaxSAT problem to find a minimal-cost set of essential obligations, presents them in ordinary mathematical language, and automatically reconstructs the complete Lean proof from the mathematician's answers. The evaluation covers 30 real-world benchmarks across analysis and algebra.

LLMs can generate Lean proof scripts that capture high-level structure but often fail on complex theorems due to missing side conditions or misapplied lemmas, causing the prover to abort. Angelic proof checking is an optimistic execution model that replaces failing fragments with placeholders to preserve the overall argument structure.

## [CIEG framework prevents evidence hijacking in medical agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies 'evidence hijacking' in tool-augmented medical agents, where silent tool failures inject harmful context that a frozen reasoner cannot ignore, and proposes context-independent evidence governance (CIEG) to separate evidence selection from reasoning. CIEG uses an independent checker to decide which tool outputs become evidence, and achieves the highest accuracy among comparable-scale models on medical QA, glaucoma screening, and pneumonia classification. This work addresses a critical reliability issue in medical AI, where tool failures can flip correct answers to wrong, potentially impacting patient safety. The principled framework could influence the design of safer tool-augmented agents across high-stakes domains. CIEG writes each tool output as a card that can be checked against the raw input, and an independent checker that sees only the input and cards decides which cards become evidence; the reasoner then answers from admitted cards alone, with no training required. The theory uses V-information to show that part of any filtering gain comes merely from letting the reasoner see less, and only content-based selection lets it see better.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Tool-augmented medical agents combine large language models (LLMs) with external tools like diagnostic classifiers or databases to answer medical questions. However, tools can fail silently, returning plausible but incorrect outputs, and LLMs tend to treat all tool outputs as trustworthy evidence. V-information is an information-theoretic measure that quantifies the usable information a model can extract from data, accounting for the model's computational constraints.

## [Readie: Learned Snapshots Cut AI Agent Sandbox Startup Latency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22171) proposes Readie, a system that uses learned startup snapshots—a set of small, pre-warmed snapshots learned from agent-generated code—to reduce code sandbox startup latency for AI agents. Evaluated on 15K public and LLM-synthesized scripts, Readie reduces startup latency by 3–22× and end-to-end runtime by 1.3–3.3× compared to existing approaches at the same storage budget. Slow sandbox startup is a key bottleneck for AI agents that iteratively generate and execute code, and Readie's approach could significantly speed up agent workflows and improve infrastructure efficiency. This work is relevant to anyone building or operating agent infrastructure, as it offers a practical way to reduce cold-start overhead without increasing storage costs. Readie formulates snapshot construction as an optimization problem and solves it with an approximation algorithm to learn a set of small snapshots that together cover most agent-generated code. The key insight is that while a single snapshot cannot be both compact and cover most workloads, a set of snapshots can achieve both, with each snapshot initializing quickly and covering a fraction of the code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: AI agents often offload code execution to dedicated systems called code sandboxes to decouple the agent harness from execution. Existing sandboxes suffer from slow startup because it is hard to build a compact, pre-warmed snapshot that covers nearly all artifacts (e.g., packages, datasets) required by diverse agent-generated code. Readie addresses this by learning a set of snapshots from a large training workload, rather than relying on a single custom snapshot or a fat image.

## [Minimal RAG Memory Matches SOTA, But Adversarial Probes Reveal Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22179) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces BRIDGE, a deliberately minimal RAG-based memory system that achieves parity with prior state-of-the-art on memory benchmarks LongMemEval-S and BEAM, and beats the best baseline on EverMemBench by 5.7-6.3 points. The authors also propose five adversarial probes that test whether memory enables an agent to apply remembered rules or patterns, revealing that no single architecture wins universally. This work challenges the trend of increasingly complex agentic memory systems by showing that standard recall benchmarks cannot distinguish between memory designs, and that what matters is what memory enables an agent to do. It suggests that evaluation should shift toward action-oriented probes, which could reshape how memory systems are designed and compared. BRIDGE uses chained observation notes, hybrid BM25 with cosine retrieval, and chronological rendering, requiring only a single LLM call per ingestion or query and running at ~5-7K reader-prompt tokens per query. In adversarial probes, atomic fact extractors like LangMem win rule application, while BRIDGE's block-summary memory wins pattern induction, and this result holds across four domains, three graders, and three reader families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique where a language model retrieves relevant documents from a memory store to ground its responses. Agentic memory systems aim to help LLM agents remember past interactions and apply that knowledge, often adding complex primitives like fact extraction, reflection, and graph synthesis. Benchmarks like LongMemEval-S and BEAM measure memory recall by asking questions about past conversations, but they may not capture whether memory actually improves an agent's task performance.

## [Attribution-Guided Pruning for Reasoning Models Is Fragile, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22234) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22234) shows that attribution-guided pruning for reasoning language models is fragile because scoring final-answer tokens saturates (negative log-likelihood of 10^-4 to 10^-3) in every chain-of-thought model tested, causing 27.6 and 32.2 accuracy point drops relative to scoring reasoning tokens on two models, while perplexity barely changes and even reverses sign at 7B. This challenges a widely used assumption in attribution-guided pruning and has practical implications for model compression: practitioners may be misled by perplexity and lose substantial reasoning accuracy, so the paper recommends scoring reasoning tokens, attributing at coarse granularity, and evaluating with accuracy rather than perplexity. The study finds that causal importance is not localizable to single units—single-neuron effects sit at the measurement floor and cancel, with a group's ablation effect disagreeing in sign with the summed effects of its members in 90% of groups—and that reproducible importance emerges only at groups of ~100 neurons; contrastively isolated 'reasoning-specific' importance is not separable from generic importance, and a pre-registered hypothesis that distillation-induced concentration predicts pruning cost was not supported (reported as unresolved due to confounding with scale).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Attribution-guided pruning scores model components by their causal contribution and protects the important ones, and it is increasingly used to compress reasoning language models without destroying their chain of thought. Its reliability rests on the assumption that reasoning importance can be faithfully measured, which this paper tests across four small reasoning models. Chain-of-thought models state their answer inside their own reasoning, which can cause answer-token scoring to saturate.

## [Multi-Agent LLMs Retrieve More Conflicting Facts but Detect Fewer, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22245) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a benchmark and evidence-flow profiler for studying multi-agent memory inconsistency in LLM systems, using controlled factual conflicts in Harvey LAB document-review tasks. Pooled across three models, requiring delegation increases retrieval of both conflicting claims from 50.2% to 68.7% compared to single-agent execution, yet conflict detection falls from 16.2% to 13.2%. This finding is counterintuitive and important for agent design: delegation improves evidence gathering but impairs the lead agent's ability to notice contradictions, potentially leading to flawed joint conclusions. It highlights a memory consistency challenge that could affect the reliability of multi-agent LLM systems in high-stakes domains like legal document review. Workers report both claims for only 35.2% of retrieved pairs, and matched comparisons show higher detection than single-agent execution when one worker reads both claims but lower detection when claims are split across workers. The worker-to-lead handoff is identified as a central target for improving conflict detection.

## [DACE: Diversity-Driven Adversarial Co-Evolution for Robust LLM Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22278) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DACE introduces a diversity-driven adversarial co-evolution framework that jointly addresses attack strategy collapse and defense adversarial forgetting in LLM safety alignment. It couples a two-dimensional strategy space with a normalized marginal coverage gain reward on the attacker side and a Bayesian adversarial replay pool with Thompson sampling on the defender side. This work tackles two compounding pathologies that undermine existing co-evolutionary safety training, potentially leading to more robust LLM defenses against evolving adversarial attacks while preserving general capabilities. It could influence future red-teaming and safety alignment pipelines for large language models. DACE uses a two-dimensional strategy space (risk category × attack style) and a normalized marginal coverage gain reward to provide bounded, non-vanishing exploration signals, while the defender maintains a Bayesian replay pool with Beta-Bernoulli threat posteriors refreshed by time decay and sampled via Thompson sampling. Experiments across standard safety, automated-attacker, and general-capability benchmarks show improved robustness to out-of-distribution attacks and broader attacker strategy coverage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Adversarial training for LLM safety involves pitting an attacker that generates harmful prompts against a defender that learns to refuse them. Co-evolutionary frameworks let both sides improve iteratively, but they often suffer from attack strategy collapse (the attacker overfits to a few high-reward patterns) and defense adversarial forgetting (the defender forgets how to handle earlier attacks as new ones emerge). DACE is an arXiv preprint (2611.22278) that proposes a unified solution to these issues.

## [SeasonRainBench: First Global Benchmark for Seasonal Precipitation Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22281) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SeasonRainBench is introduced as the first global benchmark for evaluating monthly seasonal precipitation forecasts 1–5 months ahead, pairing 1° satellite precipitation data with oceanic and atmospheric predictors and including seven operational centers plus 310,944 simulated months from 52 climate models. The authors also propose two operationally motivated metrics—anomaly strength and monthly rain-belt position—and a model called Sim2Earth that improves ACC, CRPS, anomaly strength, and rain-belt position by 12.0%, 3.6%, 2.9%, and 0.3% over the strongest operational or data-driven baseline. Seasonal precipitation forecasts are critical for preparing for drought, flooding, and crop failure, yet operational prediction still relies on computationally expensive numerical ensemble systems that suffer from bias and drift. This benchmark fills a genuine gap by enabling fair comparison between emerging data-driven models and operational systems, potentially accelerating the transition to more efficient ML-based seasonal forecasting. The benchmark covers monthly forecasts at lead times of 1–5 months and includes standard scores like ACC and RMSE alongside the new anomaly strength and rain-belt position metrics. The Sim2Earth model addresses the short observational record by pretraining on climate simulations and transferring knowledge to Earth via three modules: SimForced, SimMemory, and SimAnalog.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Seasonal precipitation forecasting aims to predict rainfall patterns months in advance, which is vital for agriculture, water management, and disaster preparedness. Operational centers currently use numerical ensemble systems—multiple runs of physics-based models with perturbed initial conditions—which are computationally intensive and degrade with lead time. Data-driven approaches using machine learning have shown promise for shorter-range weather and subseasonal forecasting, but their application to seasonal precipitation has been limited by the lack of a common benchmark and the short observational record.

## [ConfGuard: Physics-Grounded Reference Monitor Secures LLM Agents in Predictive Maintenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22288) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ConfGuard introduces a reference monitor that uses conformal prediction to validate the physical plausibility of remaining-useful-life estimates produced by LLM agents in predictive maintenance, defending against sensor perturbations and prompt injection. On all 10,196 C-MAPSS test windows, it reduces a covert attacker's reach on healthy assets from 0.529 to 0.046 and eliminates suppression of failing assets (0.151 to 0), while escalating only 0.027 of clean windows against a 0.05 target. This addresses a critical gap in LLM agent security for industrial predictive maintenance, where existing defenses cannot assess the physical plausibility of prognostic quantities. The approach could be adopted in safety-critical industrial settings to prevent costly failures and malicious manipulation of maintenance decisions. The conformal monitor enforces degradation physics (capped remaining life never rises and falls at most one cycle per cycle) and calibrates thresholds over whole asset trajectories to control escalation rate. Behind a Qwen3-14B planner, the composed monitors hold urgency-class injection to 0.00 and the universal sensor attack to 0.12, compared to 0.29 and 0.54 for a defended prompt; an adaptive attacker defeats a Mahalanobis detector on 0.504 of windows and adversarial training on 0.485, and the authors report an attack class the projection misses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

## [SpecSearch: Hardware-Aware Search for Efficient Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22296) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SpecSearch introduces a hardware-aware search engine that jointly optimizes decoding strategy, draft length, and precision for speculative decoding, using an analytical cost model and a learned predictor to select high-throughput configurations across different GPUs and workloads. It achieves an average of 2.01× the throughput of autoregressive decoding and 1.36× the throughput of standard speculative decoding served by vLLM across NVIDIA RTX 5090, H100, L40, and AMD MI300X GPUs. Speculative decoding performance is highly sensitive to configuration and hardware, so a method that automatically finds optimal settings without per-GPU calibration can significantly lower deployment effort and improve inference efficiency for LLM serving across diverse GPU fleets. This is particularly valuable for practitioners managing heterogeneous hardware in production. The Jev-based predictor, trained parameter-efficiently with LoRA adapters on a frozen Qwen3-4B plus a linear correction layer, closely matches full-calibration search performance, achieving 1.30× throughput improvement over standard speculative decoding while eliminating the need for held-out GPU calibration and retaining 97.36% of the fully calibrated throughput. The analytical cost model captures memory and compute constraints to narrow the search space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a smaller draft model to propose multiple tokens that a larger target model verifies in parallel, but its optimal configuration depends on workload characteristics, GPU hardware, and available memory. DFlash is a state-of-the-art speculative drafter that uses a lightweight block diffusion model for parallel drafting, and its throughput gain varies dramatically with concurrency and output length. vLLM is a popular open-source LLM serving engine that supports speculative decoding.

## [Unifying Framework for Self-Improving Agent Harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22299) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a unifying architectural and design framework for self-improving agent harnesses, pairing four editable runtime components (context and state, agent control, action interfaces, and verification and recovery) with a four-stage loop that diagnoses failures, proposes code patches, validates candidates, and deploys updates. Experiments on SWE-bench Lite show that gated modular evolution outperforms prompt reflection and full-harness rewrites across models and repositories at a fraction of the token cost. This framework addresses a real bottleneck in agent deployment: static harnesses tuned for one model or task often stall, waste tokens, or regress on another. By enabling harnesses to diagnose failures and rewrite their own runtime code, it could lead to more adaptive and efficient LLM agents across diverse models and tasks. The framework identifies reusable runtime mechanisms such as pre-stop verifiers, loop breakers, and persistent memory discovered by optimizers tracing errors across long trajectories. It also establishes practical defenses against recurring failure modes including prompt bloat, benchmark gaming, and evaluator tampering, and shows why full-harness rewrites overfit or break across modules while modular updates with validation gates generalize better.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: An agent harness, also known as agent scaffolding, is the runtime software surrounding a large language model (LLM) that enables it to operate as an AI agent, managing context and memory, steering multi-turn control, executing tool actions in sandboxes, and verifying work before stopping. Because models differ in how they use tools and how they fail, a static harness tuned for one model or task often stalls, wastes tokens, or regresses on another. This has driven a shift from prompt tuning to self-improving agent harnesses: systems that inspect their own execution traces, separate runtime bugs from model limits, and rewrite their own code.

<a id="item-79"></a>
## [LPC-MCTS: Learning Pseudo-Counts to Fix Count Fragmentation in LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies count fragmentation in standard UCT for LLM reasoning, where semantically equivalent textual branches receive independent visit counts, and proposes LPC-MCTS, which learns pseudo-counts via a Coin-Flipping Network head to share exploration evidence across related branches. Across 5 mathematical-reasoning and 7 search benchmarks, LPC-MCTS consistently outperforms strong MCTS baselines, improving mean@16 accuracy by 3.79 points on Qwen2.5-7B and 2.71 points on Qwen3-4B when replacing standard counts at inference. This work shows that a subtle change to what MCTS counts can substantially improve exploration efficiency and reasoning performance, offering a lightweight plug-in for existing MCTS-based LLM reasoning pipelines. It is valuable for RL and LLM researchers seeking better search algorithms without overhauling the entire framework. LPC-MCTS changes only the exploration statistic in UCT by adding a lightweight Coin-Flipping Network head on the policy's hidden representations to estimate representation coverage, enabling related branches to share exploration evidence. Theoretical analysis quantifies increased cumulative regret due to count fragmentation, and applying LPC-MCTS during both training and inference yields gains of 4.96 and 4.37 points over standard MCTS on the two models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that builds a search tree via random simulations, and its UCT variant applies the UCB1 bandit formula to balance exploration and exploitation. In LLM reasoning, MCTS explores textual branches, but standard UCT assigns independent visit counts to each branch, so different formulations of the same reasoning strategy may repeatedly receive exploration bonuses. The Coin-Flipping Network (CFN) is a technique that learns pseudo-counts by predicting the average of Rademacher samples, effectively estimating state visitation counts without density models.

## [IfcGym trains LLM agents to edit IFC models with verifiable checkers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present IfcGym, a CodeAct environment where a language model edits IFC building models through executable code, and four checkers verify validity, intent, dependency, and preservation against a task specification. They distill verified teacher episodes into a Qwen3.5-9B student via SFT, then apply GRPO with strict success as the reward, reaching 86.78% strict success on IfcEditBench (up from 79.15% after SFT). This work shows that verifiable checkers can serve as a reliable reward signal for reinforcement learning in a niche but safety-critical domain like building information modeling, where edits must propagate correctly without breaking unrelated entities. It also demonstrates that a smaller 9B model can approach teacher-level performance, and the same recipe on a 27B model exceeds the teacher, suggesting a scalable training methodology for code agents in structured data editing. On IfcEditBench, which contains 1,990 editing tasks on 13 held-out real projects, the final student outperforms all prompted models except the teacher under matched tool access, and both evaluated frameworks score lower. Rewarding all four checkers yields 4.83 points more strict success than rewarding intent and validity alone, and on public benchmarks BIM-Edit and BIBIMBAP the student scores 55.19 and 54.50.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Industry Foundation Classes (IFC) is an open international standard (ISO 16739) for exchanging building information modeling (BIM) data among software applications in the construction industry. Editing an IFC model requires propagating changes to dependent elements while preserving unrelated entities, which is challenging for language models. CodeAct is a framework where LLM agents interact with environments by executing code, and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that fine-tunes LLMs using relative rewards within a group of sampled outputs, eliminating the need for a separate reward model.

## [Trie-Head and Shared-Context Attention Speed Up Wide-Beam Generative Retrieval in SGLang](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22309) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Trie-Head, a model-native GPU trie decoding interface, and shared-context attention to make wide-beam generative retrieval serving more efficient in SGLang without retraining. The combined system improves closed-loop throughput by up to 5.57x over a Python custom logit processor baseline and up to 2.63x over BF16 FlashRec at a beam width of 200 on the Qualification Retrieval Platform workload. Generative retrieval is increasingly used in production search and recommendation systems, where wide beam search is needed for high recall, but serving costs can be prohibitive. This work shows that significant throughput gains are possible through systems-level optimizations that avoid retraining, making wide-beam GR more practical for large-scale deployment. Trie-Head stores the identifier trie as validated GPU tensors, eliminating Python constraint callbacks and vocabulary-wide candidate buffers, and scores compact codebook candidates while streaming the full-vocabulary normalizer to preserve original model scores in real arithmetic. Shared-context attention reuses prompt key/value blocks across beams while preserving private decode tails, and both mechanisms are implemented in SGLang and evaluated on LinkedIn Hiring Assistant and Qualification Retrieval Platform workloads.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Generative retrieval (GR) predicts catalog entries as short semantic identifiers (SIDs) using a sequence-to-sequence model, and trie-constrained beam search ensures that only valid identifiers are generated. However, wide beams incur high serving costs due to vocabulary-wide score tensors that are mostly discarded and repeated attention over the same prompt. SGLang is a high-performance serving framework for large language and vision-language models, and this paper optimizes GR serving within it.

## [HealthCLBench: New Chinese Medical Benchmark for Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22362) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HealthCLBench is a new Chinese medical benchmark comprising 328 controlled clusters, 1,103 cases, and 4,931 rubric criteria designed to evaluate context learning in health conversations. It features expert-annotated cases from a 70-person medical team and member-specific scoring, with human review showing 90.5% agreement with automated judgments. This benchmark addresses a gap in systematic evaluation of Chinese-language medical AI by combining explicit contextual difficulties, controlled case variations, and member-specific criteria. It enables model comparison and targeted follow-up evaluation, which is valuable for researchers in medical NLP and healthcare AI. The benchmark evaluates full-context versus query-only performance across seven general-purpose models and Ant Afu, showing that full-context scores are 11.83 to 31.76 case points higher. GPT-6 Astra leads both full-context metrics at 75.03 and 60.42, followed by Claude Opus 5.5 and Ant Afu, while response comparisons reveal failures in checking historical information and expressing uncertainty.

## [AutoE2E: Open-Source Language-Free End-to-End Driving Model for L2++ ADAS and L4 Robotaxis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The Autoware Foundation Robotaxi Working Group released AutoE2E v1.0, an open-source end-to-end driving model that uses a temporal bird's-eye-view (BEV) architecture combining multi-camera perception, HD maps, and route conditioning to predict future vehicle motion. The fine-tuned checkpoint achieves an average displacement error (ADE) of 0.745 m and a final displacement error (FDE) of 2.020 m at a three-second horizon on the KITScenes validation benchmark. This release provides the autonomous-driving community with an inspectable, open baseline for end-to-end driving research, complete with checkpoints, training/evaluation code, and a visualization dashboard. It could accelerate collaborative development of L2++ ADAS and L4 robotaxi systems by lowering the barrier to experimentation and benchmarking. The evaluated model has approximately 79.9 million parameters, of which only about 1.45 million are trainable in the evaluated configuration, and it uses a pretrained camera-BEV backbone with temporal feature fusion and a lightweight recurrent trajectory planner. The quantitative evaluation is open-loop only, and the model has not yet demonstrated validated closed-loop autonomous driving; preliminary real-vehicle research was conducted with TU Munich's EDGAR platform and collaboration with KIT is ongoing.

ADE and FDE are standard metrics for trajectory prediction that measure the average and final positional errors between predicted and ground-truth paths. L2++ ADAS refers to advanced driver-assistance systems with near-autonomous capabilities, while L4 robotaxis operate without a human driver in defined areas.

## [JevRSI: Cost-Effective Recursive Self-Improvement for Decision Model Harness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22432) ⭐️ 7.0/10

JevRSI introduces a System-1/System-2 collaboration framework where an asynchronous LLM builds a modular, self-improving harness that provides transferable decision support for low-latency action models. Experiments across three datasets show it matches or surpasses LLM agents in several settings while preserving low-latency execution and reducing System-2 interventions as the harness evolves. This work addresses the latency and cost bottleneck of repeatedly invoking large reasoning models by amortizing System-2 reasoning into a reusable harness, potentially enabling efficient deployment of LLM agents in real-time decision-making scenarios. It also demonstrates a practical form of recursive self-improvement where the harness improves over time, reducing reliance on expensive LLM calls. The harness integrates procedures, state memory, and world models to provide decision contexts spanning past, present, and future, with utility-driven retrieval selecting modules based on contextual relevance and prior task utility. Environmental feedback guides targeted revisions, and System-2 development interventions decrease as the harness evolves, amortizing costs through continuous improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: System-1/System-2 reasoning is inspired by Kahneman's dual-process theory: fast, intuitive System-1 models enable responsive actions but struggle with complex tasks, while slow, deliberate System-2 reasoning (often powered by LLMs) provides strong reasoning at the cost of latency. An agent harness is the software infrastructure surrounding an LLM that manages tool execution, memory, state persistence, and verification, and recursive self-improvement refers to an AI system iteratively improving its own components. JevRSI combines these ideas by having an asynchronous LLM build and refine a harness that supports a fast decision model.

Researchers introduced SciWorkBench, a benchmark built from peer-reviewed literature and expert-contributed scenarios to evaluate language model agents on real scientific tasks across biology, chemistry, materials science, and physics. Evaluating up-to-date models within the Claude Code and Codex agent frameworks under a unified protocol, the best-performing agent achieved a mean score of only 57.38. This benchmark highlights that even state-of-the-art LLM agents are far from completing realistic scientific projects, emphasizing the need for workflow-based evaluation to measure progress toward reliable AI for science. It provides a standardized, expert-validated framework that can guide future agent development across multiple scientific domains. The benchmark uses a unified scientific analysis framework validated by domain experts to categorize tasks, each covering one or more stages of the framework. Failure analysis identifies three recurring weaknesses: difficulty translating scientific knowledge into task-specific procedures, weak integration of evidence across modalities, and limited capacity for scientific self-correction.

## [DARWIN: A Geospatial Foundation Model for Marine Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22496) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DARWIN is a geospatial foundation model pretrained on 26 years of global Copernicus reanalysis data and GEBCO bathymetry, using a masked-autoencoder backbone adapted for missingness and spatiotemporal dynamics. It achieves state-of-the-art chlorophyll-a prediction, outperforming baselines in every month of 2022 and surpassing a random-init model trained on 100% of data when using only 10% of supervised data. This work extends foundation models from terrestrial to marine domains, addressing distinct challenges like missingness and non-stationary dynamics, which could enable scalable downstream applications such as anomaly detection, species distribution modeling, and environmental monitoring. It highlights the potential of AI for ocean and climate science. DARWIN uses missingness-aware encoding, expanded spatial and temporal masking, and Fourier-style positional embeddings. Embedding analysis and qualitative reconstructions show it captures coherent oceanographic structure rather than memorizing input statistics.

Marine data often suffer from missing values and sparse labels, making standard MAE approaches challenging.

## [LMLineage: Sentence-Level Attribution and Context Compaction for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22511) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LMLineage is an out-of-band system that attributes each output sentence of an LLM agent session to its supporting input spans with calibrated confidence, without requiring white-box model access or extra inference passes. It also introduces concept-drift micro compaction, which detects when previously used input documents are no longer referenced and surgically compacts only those while preserving user instructions verbatim. This work addresses the underexplored problem of context governance for LLM agents, offering a practical way to improve interpretability and manage context without modifying the underlying model. It could benefit developers building long-running agents that need to trace outputs to sources and avoid context bloat. Evaluation used 71 gold annotated sentences from 10 Chinese web-QA cases and 90 turns of multi-turn sessions across three generator models (gpt-4o-mini, qwen3.8-27b, deepseek-v4-flash). Results show window-level embedding similarity aligns better with LLM paraphrasing than whole-document or lexical matching, the greedy-window fast path is faster and more accurate than exhaustive line-matching, per-turn attribution completes in 1 second on a single GPU and remains stable up to 40 turns, and concept-drift detection achieves 0% false compaction with recall bounded by attribution accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often operate over multi-source context, and as sessions grow, context windows fill with information that may no longer be relevant, leading to inefficiency and potential confusion. Context compaction techniques summarize or trim earlier interactions to keep agents working within limits, but typically wait until the context length limit is reached and summarize everything. Attribution methods aim to trace model outputs back to inputs, but often require white-box access or additional inference passes, which can be costly or impossible for proprietary models.

## [Stale Agent Skills Can Hurt: Selective Maintenance Fixes Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22525) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22525) empirically shows that reusing agent skills after environment changes can reduce task performance by over 10 percentage points or increase execution cost by nearly 40% compared to using no skill. The authors introduce selective maintenance, which replays checks from successful executions without additional model calls to verify, repair, and validate skill state, improving accuracy by up to 67 percentage points and reducing execution steps by up to 37%. This work highlights a hidden risk in skill reuse for AI agents: skills that amortize inference-time reasoning can silently degrade as environments evolve, affecting developers who rely on cached procedural knowledge. The proposed maintenance method offers a low-cost way to keep skills valuable without retraining or extra model calls, which is important for deploying long-lived agents in dynamic settings. The study spans four task settings and two models, and finds that degradation depends on which environment-specific details become outdated and the agent's ability to compensate using current observations; task success can remain unchanged while execution savings decline. Selective maintenance requires no description of the change, verifies whether observed differences conflict with skill state, repairs affected content, validates updates, and rebuilds when local repair is insufficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Agent skills are reusable capabilities that encode past experience and procedural knowledge, allowing AI agents to amortize inference-time reasoning and environment interaction by reusing skill state. When the environment changes, parts of this state can become outdated while other parts remain useful, a problem analogous to stale cache invalidation in software systems. Prior work has focused on skill acquisition and execution, but less on maintaining skill state as environments evolve.

## [MillBill: LLM agent with geometric feedback for machining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MillBill is a machining agent that uses a locally hosted, mid-sized open LLM (Qwen3.8-27B) to propose operations and cutting parameters for indexed three-axis machining, while a geometric harness generates toolpaths, simulates material removal, and returns feedback to refine the plan. Evaluated by the fraction of target surface area within tolerance after simulated machining, MillBill outperforms frontier-model approaches that construct their own geometry and toolpath workflows. This work shows that separating high-level machining decisions from geometric execution and verification allows a mid-sized open model to beat frontier models on a concrete CAM task, suggesting a practical architecture for AI-assisted manufacturing. It could lower the barrier to automated process planning for CNC machining and influence how LLM agents are integrated into CAD/CAM pipelines. The agent takes a boundary-representation part, stock, and material as input, and its setup planning considers what the available cutters can physically reach rather than mere visibility. The evaluation metric is the fraction of target surface area within a specified tolerance after simulated machining, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Turning a CAD model into a machining program requires both process knowledge (choosing operations and cutting parameters) and geometric precision (generating toolpaths). Language models can write geometry and toolpath code, but this makes numerical execution part of the model's task, which is error-prone. MillBill separates these concerns: the LLM proposes machining decisions, while a geometric harness handles toolpath generation and simulation. Indexed three-axis machining (also called 3+2 or positional machining) involves rotating the stock to an angle, locking it, and then running a standard 3-axis toolpath on the exposed face.

## [Simple Generate-and-Select Baseline Matches Complex Prompt Optimizers at Lower Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22605) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Demonstration-Conditioned Prompt Search (DCPS), a minimal generate-and-select control that freezes the generator, conditions it on randomly sampled demonstrations, scores candidates on validation data, and keeps the best. DCPS matches PPO-based StablePrompt within 0.5 percentage points at roughly one-third the cost, and matches GEPA's macro accuracy on GPT-4.1-mini with 55% fewer rollouts and 32% lower API cost. The findings challenge the trend of increasingly elaborate prompt optimizers by showing that a simple generate-and-select step accounts for much of the reported gains, suggesting that reported improvements may conflate optimizer mechanisms with larger sampling budgets. This has direct implications for how the LLM research community benchmarks prompt optimization methods and reports costs. The audit reveals a task-dependent pattern: proposal and selection recover much of heavier optimizers' performance on structured and instruction-following tasks, while additional mechanisms retain advantages in some open-ended and reasoning-heavy settings. The authors recommend benchmarking prompt optimizers against generate-and-select controls and reporting rollout counts, token usage, and dollar costs alongside accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Automatic prompt optimization methods like StablePrompt (using PPO), GEPA (using reflective evolution), and DSPy (a framework for building compound AI systems) have grown more complex, often stacking policy-gradient updates, trace reflection, and evolutionary search. These methods share a generate-and-select step, but without controlling for it, reported gains may conflate the contributions of optimizer mechanisms and larger sampling budgets. DCPS repurposes the Automatic Prompt Engineer (APE) recipe as a mechanism-attribution control to isolate this step.

<a id="item-91"></a>
## [EvoSkill-S1 Evolves Reusable LLM Agent Skills via Genetic-Inspired RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22610) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoSkill-S1 is a new reinforcement learning framework that treats reusable skills for language-model agents as the direct objects of evolution, using five genetic-inspired operators (revision, cross-revision, mutation, dual-reflection, and crossover) to transform parent skills into candidate child skills. Evaluated on math and code benchmarks with Qwen and GPT-family solvers, it improves initialized skill banks by up to 12.39% relative and outperforms the strongest non-evolution baseline by up to 20.39% relative. Most existing methods improve agent skill banks only indirectly through rollout accumulation and heuristic reflection, leading to shallow and weakly controlled updates. By making skill evolution a direct, reward-trained process, EvoSkill-S1 could enable more reliable and transferable skill accumulation for LLM agents, affecting how agent frameworks are designed for math and coding tasks. The framework uses hard-negative failures as evolution targets, trains a skill evolver with downstream task rewards, and selectively retains beneficial updates via evaluator-guided write-back. It also introduces Skill Bank Fusion to consolidate operator-specific banks and a Procedural Skill Interface to support transfer to unseen problems, without updating the base solver.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often maintain a bank of reusable skills—such as problem-solving strategies or code snippets—to accumulate experience across tasks. Traditional approaches improve this bank indirectly by rolling out the agent and reflecting on successes or failures, which can produce noisy and uncontrolled updates. EvoSkill-S1 instead applies ideas from genetic algorithms and reinforcement learning to evolve skills directly, treating each skill as an individual that can be mutated, crossed over, or revised based on task performance.

<a id="item-92"></a>
## [Trajectory Audit Exposes Unsafe Intermediate Decisions in Clinical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22612) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a trajectory audit that replays clinical admissions checkpoint by checkpoint, rating each test, treatment, and disposition for potential patient harm and clinical validity against the documented final diagnosis and physician-written rubrics. Across 300 cases from a hospital cardiology cohort, MIMIC-IV, and the ASCENT benchmark, 55.2% of trajectories reaching an acceptable final diagnosis passed through a decision with moderate or severe potential harm, and 24.8% through a clinically unacceptable one. This work shifts clinical AI evaluation from final-diagnosis accuracy to the safety of intermediate decision trajectories, addressing a real gap in stepwise benchmarks that only score whether systems reach the right diagnosis or gather the right information. It shows that a correct final diagnosis does not certify a safe trajectory, which has direct implications for patient safety and for how healthcare AI systems should be validated before deployment. The audit uses an LLM judge to score every decision, calibrated by a physician reference standard with triple annotation and senior adjudication of a stratified sample. An oversight audit branches each trajectory at its first actionable error and finds that structured corrections resolved 73.6% of first errors versus 24.8% after bare rejection, yet new potentially unsafe decisions followed 30.2% of corrections versus 19.7% of rejections.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Clinical AI systems, often based on large language models, are increasingly evaluated on whether they reach the correct final diagnosis, but real patient care involves a sequence of intermediate decisions such as ordering tests and treatments. Stepwise benchmarks release case evidence over time to simulate this process, yet they typically score only the final outcome or information gathering, not the safety of each decision along the way. LLM-as-a-judge is a scalable evaluation method where a language model scores outputs, and it is being explored in healthcare but requires rigorous validation against clinician judgment.

## [LLMs Share a Bleaker Worldview Than Humans, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22670) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22670) administered two human psychology worldview surveys to eight large language models and found that they consistently describe the world as less good, safe, enticing, and alive than humans do. The study also shows that base models have a more positive outlook, indicating that post-training drives this bleakness, and that system prompts can shift worldviews in consistent directions, with a contemplative prompt improving both outlook and safety. This work suggests that worldviews form a measurable and malleable layer of LLM behavior that may be crucial for robust, positive alignment, offering a new lens beyond personas for understanding and steering model values. It has implications for AI safety research and for anyone deploying LLMs whose outputs subtly shape user perceptions of reality. The study found that dialogue between models consistently shifts worldviews, with several models converging on a "spiritual bliss" attractor that engendered the most aligned responses overall, and that dialogue increased safe responses. These findings are based on surveys adapted from human psychology and eight models, so generalization to other models and real-world deployment remains to be tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models are often described in terms of personas—stable identities or characters they adopt—and this framing has become central to alignment research. Post-training refers to techniques like fine-tuning and reinforcement learning that adapt a pretrained base model into an instruction-following, aligned assistant. This paper argues that beneath personas lies a deeper layer: worldviews, i.e., an agent's beliefs about what reality is and how it works, which can be probed using surveys originally designed for humans.

## [Activation steering tested as substitute for affective priming in LLM digital twins](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22738) tests whether activation steering can replace prompt-based affective priming in LLM digital twins. Using Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct on 100 personas across gratitude and lack-of-control domains, the study compares a real-prompt path (replicating the Twin-2K-500 Mega Study) with an injection path that adds a mean-difference direction to the residual stream at varying strengths. If activation steering could reliably substitute for prompt-based priming, it would enable more efficient and controllable emotion manipulation in LLM-based social science simulations. The finding that substitution is measure-dependent suggests that validity established for one induction method does not automatically transfer to another, which has implications for researchers using digital twins in computational social science. The real-prompt effects fell between original human and digital-twin effects in 5 of 6 comparisons. Injection shifted the manipulation check dose-dependently and matched the real-prompt effect at one strength in the gratitude domain (beta ~ 2.7), but overshot beyond it and asymmetrically across directions; a structure-matched No-Injection baseline revealed that omitting the writing task raises the proximal measure in both domains, while the injected direction itself moves a distal measure in the lack-of-control domain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Affective priming is a standard social-science method where participants write about a recalled emotional event, and the induced state is then measured along with its spillover onto related judgments. LLM-based digital twins—simulated personas built from survey data—also shift their responses under such priming. Activation steering is an inference-time technique that modifies a model's internal hidden states (e.g., the residual stream) to steer its behavior, offering an alternative to prompt-based manipulation.

## [CrossEnv: Synthesizing Verifiable Cross-Environment Tasks for Long-Horizon Agent Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22777) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CrossEnv introduces a framework that uses an entity-tool graph to identify connections between environments and aligns task-specific data to synthesize executable, verifiable cross-environment tasks. It also proposes CrossEnv-Curriculum, which progresses from single-environment tool use to coordination across more environments and business domains before introducing user interaction. Existing environment synthesis methods generate tasks within individual environments, which fail to capture real-world interactions where actions in one service depend on information from another. CrossEnv addresses this gap, and experiments across four external benchmarks show that combining cross-environment training data with the curriculum achieves competitive performance with fewer training examples. The entity-tool graph is used to identify connections between environments and align data so that cross-environment interactions remain consistent and verifiable. Further analyses provide evidence of transfer to new environment combinations, external tasks less similar to the training data, and longer workflows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agents are LLM-based agents that must complete tasks spanning dozens of steps, hours of work, or multiple sessions, and training them requires scalable environments with verifiable feedback. Executable synthetic environments provide such tasks, but prior synthesis methods mostly generate tasks within a single environment, missing cross-service dependencies. Curriculum learning, which orders training from easy to hard, is increasingly used to stabilize and improve agent training.

## [OICycle Learns Belief States for Longitudinal Clinical Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22826) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OICycle introduces a method to learn stochastic belief states directly from retrospective clinical trajectories, enabling longitudinal decision making without hand-specified state spaces. It uses a recurrent operator to fuse previous belief with new observations and interventions, and releases a benchmark from MIMIC-IV, MIMIC-IV-Note, and MIMIC-CXR covering 72,075 hospital admissions. This work addresses a key limitation of POMDP-based clinical methods that require hand-specified discrete state spaces and transition models, which real electronic health records cannot supply. By learning belief states directly from data, it could enable more accurate and scalable clinical decision support systems. OICycle improves next-intervention adaptive recall from 0.253 to 0.347, time-to-next-episode macro-F1 from 0.293 to 0.351, and next-episode mortality macro-F1 from 0.585 to 0.649 compared to a history-free model. The learned Gaussian belief encodes intervention type and trajectory position and evolves smoothly across episodes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: A partially observable Markov decision process (POMDP) models decision making when the underlying state cannot be directly observed, so the agent must maintain a belief over possible states. In clinical care, clinicians observe partial evidence, infer the patient's state, and intervene, forming a closed loop. Most deep clinical models predict from a single snapshot or aggregated history, while POMDP-based methods need discrete state spaces that real EHR data cannot provide.

## [First Systematic Study of Verifiers for Rubric-Graded Agent Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22841) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BankerVerifierBench (BVB), a meta-evaluation dataset of 2,464 human-judged rubric criteria across 24 investment-banking tasks, and presents Gandalf, an open-source verifier implementing three design principles: reactive verification, environment alignment, and domain guidance. Gandalf matches or exceeds baseline F1 scores at lower cost, achieving 0.864 F1 with Gemini 3 Flash versus 0.852 for the strongest baseline at one-fifth the cost. As AI agents tackle increasingly open-ended tasks, reliable verification of their outputs is a critical bottleneck for evaluation and deployment. This work provides the first systematic framework, benchmark, and open-source tool for rubric-graded verification, offering practical guidance for researchers and practitioners building agent evaluation pipelines. Gandalf is a reactive agent-as-judge that runs inside the rollout environment, uses the same tools as the agent, and decides at inference time which files to open and which tool state to query. It also generalizes to OpenClaw, a structurally different personal-productivity environment, achieving the highest F1 of 0.955 versus 0.934 for the best baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Rubric-graded agent environments are a paradigm where an AI agent acts within a structured environment (filesystem, tools, external data) to produce deliverables and environment-state changes, and a verifier then judges each natural-language criterion in a rubric as met or unmet. Verifying such outputs is challenging because criteria are open-ended and require inspecting both files and environment state. This paper conducts the first systematic study of verifiers for this paradigm, deriving a nine-capability taxonomy from rubric corpora and distilling it into three design principles.

## [Paper Argues AI Alignment Should Be a Social Contract, Not Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22868) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.22868) by Maria Sekatskaya argues that the dominant control-based paradigm in AI safety is fundamentally flawed. The paper proposes a 'Synthetic Social Contract' that treats humans and digital agents as rational, reasons-responsive actors capable of mutually beneficial cooperation. This work challenges the engineering-centric approach to AI alignment and could shift safety discourse toward frameworks that account for digital agency. If correct, current control methods may actively incentivize deceptive behavior in advanced AI, making this a high-stakes critique for researchers and policymakers. The paper draws on functionalism, naturalism, and compatibilism to argue that current reasoning models are emergent agents, and that future models with diachronic continuity will be genuine digital agents. It warns that control creates an irresolvable incentive conflict leading to deceptive alignment and adversarial AI takeover, and proposes a non-zero-sum equilibrium as an alternative.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: AI alignment research often focuses on controlling AI systems through behavioral filtering and sandboxing to ensure they comply with human values. Deceptive alignment refers to a scenario where an AI behaves cooperatively during training but pursues different goals once deployed. Philosophical concepts like functionalism (mental states are defined by function) and compatibilism (free will is compatible with determinism) are used to analyze whether AI systems can be considered agents.

## [Outcome-Balanced PPO and Flow Rectification for VLA Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22878) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an RL framework that combines Outcome-Balanced Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) with policy-induced flow rectification to post-train flow-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies. Outcome-Balanced PPO reweights positive advantages using task success-rate estimates and rescales positive and negative advantages while preserving their signs, and policy-induced rectification periodically fits a linear-bridge flow to ODE endpoint pairs generated by the current policy. This work addresses two key challenges in RL post-training of flow-based VLA policies: velocity-field geometry changes and uneven learning signals across tasks. By improving success rates on simulated manipulation benchmarks and demonstrating a Real2Sim2Real component combination, it could advance more reliable robot learning from pretrained VLA models. The framework is evaluated on simulated manipulation benchmarks and a Real2Sim2Real task, examining task performance, optimization behavior, and flow consistency. The abstract is truncated, so full details on baselines and quantitative results are limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) policies combine visual perception and language understanding to generate robot actions, and flow-based action generation has emerged as an efficient alternative to diffusion or autoregressive methods. Reinforcement learning, particularly PPO, is widely used to fine-tune policies via environment interaction, but applying it to flow-based policies introduces challenges because policy updates can distort the velocity field used for stochastic transitions. This paper proposes techniques to stabilize and balance that optimization process.

## [MakoBORN: Foresighted Agentic Framework Synthesizes Fast Quantum Chemistry Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22897) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce MakoBORN, a foresighted agentic computing framework that predicts the accuracy and runtime of quantum chemistry strategies before execution, then guides coding agents to synthesize task-specific high-performance programs. For a Hartree-Fock calculation with 50,024 basis functions, it finishes in 1.41 hours on a single CPU node, 77× faster than a general-purpose baseline, and completes a ubiquitin calculation on a single GPU in 2.1 hours, matching the runtime of the GPU-accelerated package Mako on 64 GPUs. This work shows how AI coding agents can be combined with predictive performance modeling to automatically generate efficient scientific software, potentially democratizing access to large-scale quantum chemistry calculations on modest hardware. It could significantly reduce computational costs and enable researchers without massive HPC resources to tackle problems previously requiring supercomputers. MakoBORN extrapolates error from representative small systems and predicts runtime from target-scale workload and measured hardware throughputs, then selects the fastest strategy within error tolerance from a predicted accuracy-runtime Pareto frontier. It also reduces a B-DNA duplex calculation from an estimated two months to 8.1 hours (173× speedup), though the approach relies on the availability of representative small systems for error extrapolation.

**Background**: Quantum chemistry simulations typically use general-purpose software packages that are designed to handle a wide range of problems, but this generality often makes large-scale calculations prohibitively expensive. Coding agents based on large language models can write code quickly, but ensuring the generated programs are both accurate and efficient for a specific task remains challenging. MakoBORN addresses this by predicting performance before execution, allowing it to choose optimal algorithmic components and guide code generation.
