## [Anytime Evaluation Reveals When Coding Agents Should Stop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22901) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces anytime evaluation to measure the value of continued work by coding agents after their self-tests pass, replaying about 6,000 runs of eight open models on SWE-bench Verified. It finds that the hindsight saving from stopping at passing self-tests ranges from under 1% to 57% depending on model and scaffold, and a stop-after-two-passes rule saves 23% in a preregistered replication, though the quality non-inferiority test was not established. This work quantifies a hidden cost in coding agents: they often keep working after tests pass, and the value of that extra work varies widely. The findings can help teams design early-stopping policies that cut compute costs, but the uncertain quality preservation warns that stopping too early may risk patch correctness. The study uses replay-based anytime evaluation to score intermediate patches with hidden tests and measure cost at each step, enabling paired comparisons without new trajectories. Fine-tuning agents to imitate the stopping rule saves 30% over six seeds, but quality preservation remains uncertain, and the preregistered quality test did not establish non-inferiority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Coding agents are LLM-based systems that autonomously edit code to fix issues, often running self-tests and continuing to iterate after tests pass. SWE-bench Verified is a benchmark of real GitHub issues used to evaluate such agents, and scaffolds are the surrounding infrastructure that manages tool use, memory, and verification. Anytime evaluation is a methodology that scores intermediate outputs at each step, allowing analysis of when an agent should stop.

## [VITAL: A Risk-Adjusted Validation Framework for Agentic AI in Pharma Clinical Trials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22973) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VITAL (Validation by Intended use, Traceability, Autonomy and Lifecycle evidence), a risk-adjusted validation framework for agentic AI in pharmaceutical drug development and clinical trial operations. It was developed through landscape analysis, regulatory and standards mapping, expert interviews, and multi-stakeholder refinement, and was applied across four use cases: epidemiology and treatment-landscape analysis, site selection, enrolment rescue, and trial-design trade-off support. Agentic AI systems in high-stakes regulated domains like pharma can produce plausible outputs that conceal failures in retrieval, reasoning, orchestration, or human-control points, and current assurance approaches do not establish whether evidence is sufficient to validate and govern them. VITAL offers a structured way to translate autonomy, reversibility, transparency, and patient impact into proportionate validation activities and auditable evidence, which could inform regulatory expectations and lifecycle accountability for higher-risk pharmaceutical uses. VITAL integrates context of use, controls, behavioural validation, and lifecycle evidence within a risk-adjusted framework, and enables auditable reconstruction of system behaviour. The authors note that evaluation should assess reproducibility across assessors, organisations, and system configurations, and that the framework may inform evidence requirements for higher-risk pharmaceutical uses.

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to AI systems that can autonomously plan and execute multi-step tasks, often using tools and orchestrating other models. In pharmaceutical drug development and clinical trials, such systems are increasingly used for tasks like site selection and enrolment support, but they operate in a highly regulated environment where validation and governance are critical. Traditional assurance approaches, such as those used for deterministic software or standalone AI models, may not adequately address the emergent, multi-step behaviour of agents, especially when reference standards are unavailable.

<a id="item-103"></a>
## [PhysScope: Inference-Time Visual Harness for Physics Reasoning in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23010) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PhysScope introduces an inference-time visual harness that enables vision-language models to link physical scopes directly to visual observations and self-correct premise errors without updating model weights. On the PhysAlign benchmark, it achieves higher solving scores than direct solving across all six evaluated VLMs, and with Gemini-3.8-flash it outperforms Canvas-CoT by 9.34 percentage points while also improving accuracy on the out-of-distribution PhysUniBench benchmark. This work shows that multimodal physics reasoning can be substantially improved at inference time without retraining, which lowers the barrier for deploying more reliable VLMs in educational and scientific applications. The scope-aware self-correction mechanism addresses a systematic failure mode—premise errors propagating through calculations—that affects any VLM interpreting physics diagrams. PhysScope imposes explicit constraints on state distinctions, conditions, and physical scopes during reasoning, and checks agent-proposed revisions against sources and scopes; accepted revisions invalidate registered dependents to maintain scope-aware working memory. Ablation studies confirm that explicit scope management and dependency maintenance are key design principles for multimodal physics reasoning agents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as GPT-4V, Gemini, and Claude can jointly interpret images and text, but they often misapply physical constraints outside the conditions where those constraints hold, causing premise errors that cascade through calculations. PhysAlign is a benchmark for evidence-grounded role alignment in multimodal physics reasoning, and PhysUniBench is an out-of-distribution benchmark used to test generalization. Inference-time methods aim to improve model performance without changing weights, often through prompting, tool use, or structured reasoning scaffolds.

## [PeriaTrace: AI framework for tooth-level diagnosis and patient-friendly explanations from dental X-rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23012) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PeriaTrace is a three-stage framework that performs tooth-level diagnosis, visually grounded report generation, and patient-friendly explanations from periapical radiographs, validated on 12,877 images with high accuracy and external clinical cohorts. This work addresses the time-consuming nature of interpreting periapical radiographs and report writing, and bridges the gap between technical findings and patient understanding by providing plain-language explanations and visual cues, potentially improving clinical workflow and patient communication. The framework achieved a macro Dice coefficient of 85.35%, BLEU-4 of 0.6504, F1 of 94.37% for joint tooth localization and FDI identification, and grounding-tag F1 of 77.93%; external senior endodontists considered 91.16% and 84.52% of reports suitable for retention or revision without complete rewriting, and generation averaged 4.41 seconds per radiograph.

**Background**: Periapical radiographs are intraoral X-rays that show individual teeth and surrounding bone, essential for endodontic diagnosis. The FDI World Dental Federation notation is a globally used tooth numbering system that identifies teeth by quadrant and position. The Dice coefficient is a common metric for evaluating segmentation accuracy in medical imaging, measuring overlap between predicted and ground truth regions.

## [ForecastGym: A Replayable Benchmark for LLM Forecasting Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23045) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForecastGym records what four commercial web search APIs return for 150 open prediction-market questions every two days, producing 614 evidence states and 131k search results that can be replayed offline after outcomes are known. This allows fair comparison of LLM forecasting agents' models, memory strategies, and search sources under a fixed evidence-acquisition policy. Live evaluations of forecasting agents confound retrieval with reasoning, and retrospective search can leak future information, so ForecastGym provides a controlled, replayable benchmark that isolates the model's reasoning from the evidence it sees. This addresses real methodological issues in evaluating LLM agents and could become a standard for fair comparisons in the AI/ML evaluation community. The first release covers 150 questions, 614 evidence states, and 131k search results; rerunning the same queries after settlement returns results that all but state the outcome for up to 92% of questions despite date filters. On frozen evidence, all tested LLMs trail the market in forecasting accuracy, and switching the search API changes a model's Brier score by up to 0.043, more than the spread across models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Prediction markets aggregate crowd forecasts into prices for real-world events, and LLM agents are increasingly used to forecast such events by searching the web and reasoning over retrieved evidence. However, evaluating these agents is challenging because live evaluations mix retrieval with reasoning, and retrospective search can leak later information through date filters or today's ranking of historical pages. Benchmarks like ForecastGym aim to provide a hermetic, repeatable environment by freezing evidence and replaying it offline.

## [Deep RL Controller Deployed in Real Home for Heat Pump and Underfloor Heating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23063) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents an end-to-end case study that develops, evaluates, and deploys a deep reinforcement learning (DRL) controller for an integrated underfloor heating (UFH) and heat pump (HP) system in a real occupied residential building. The best-performing agent, trained in a digital twin, was deployed without additional online fine-tuning and successfully maintained indoor temperatures within comfort limits while dynamically adjusting HP supply temperature and UFH valve positions. This work provides rare empirical evidence that DRL controllers can be transferred from simulation to real occupied buildings, addressing a key gap in building energy management. It could encourage wider adoption of data-driven control for heat pump systems, which are central to decarbonizing heating and reducing energy consumption. The study first conducted a simulation-based pilot to compare DRL algorithms and configurations, then built a digital twin of the real building for training, and finally deployed the best agent in the physical building without online fine-tuning. The results highlight robustness but also note practical challenges in transferring control policies from digital environments to real heating systems.

Deep reinforcement learning offers a data-driven approach that can learn control policies to simultaneously optimize energy consumption and thermal comfort. Digital twins—virtual replicas of physical systems—allow training DRL agents safely in simulation before real-world deployment.

## [Values in Instruction Files Shape Self-Improving Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23067) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.23067) investigates how embedding short value statements from Schwartz's theory of basic human values into a self-improving coding agent's persistent instruction file affects both its performance and the solutions it produces. Testing four higher-order value types—openness to change, conservation, self-enhancement, and self-transcendence—across diverse coding tasks, the study finds that values significantly influence both outcomes. This work highlights that value alignment in AI agents is not just an ethical concern but also a practical design lever that can improve performance and shape the character of generated code. It suggests that agent designers should carefully consider the values they embed, as these can lead to better task outcomes and more desirable solution approaches. The study found that openness values increased performance on held-out data, while conservation values led to scores below those of an agent with no values. Values also changed the size, complexity, and approach of solution algorithms, and allowing the agent to revise its own values reduced differences between value sets, indicating that human oversight is still needed for value design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Schwartz's theory of basic human values is a widely validated psychological model identifying ten universal values that motivate human behavior. Self-improving coding agents are AI systems that can edit their own code or instructions to enhance performance over time, often using persistent instruction files that provide standing context across sessions. This paper bridges these concepts by examining how human-like values in such files affect agent behavior.

<a id="item-108"></a>
## [Resolution Hypothesis: Agent Evolution Stalls Due to Noisy Evaluation, Not Weak Proposals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23068) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.23068) introduces the Resolution Hypothesis, arguing that agent evolution stalls because noisy evaluations cannot reliably distinguish helpful candidate artifacts from harmful ones. The authors support this with experiments across seven evolution methods and six models, involving over 10 million LLM calls, showing that candidate selection performs no better than random and that method rankings often rest on luck. This finding shifts the focus of self-improving agent research from designing better proposal mechanisms to making true improvements detectable and accumulable. It implies that many published gains in agent evolution may be illusory, urging the community to prioritize evaluation reliability and reproducibility. At the candidate level, helpful artifacts exist but selection is no better than random at telling them from harmful ones, causing errors to accumulate across rounds. At the method level, typical performance margins of a few percentage points are smaller than the variance between two independent runs of the same method, so published rankings often rest on luck.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Agent evolution is a paradigm where a proposer generates candidate artifacts (such as prompts, skills, or memories) and a selection mechanism accepts those that score higher, forming a self-improving loop. Prior work like Multi-Agent Evolve and EvolveR has explored such loops, but practitioners have observed inconsistent results across tasks and papers. This paper diagnoses the root cause as noisy evaluation rather than weak proposal generation.

Colombo is a self-organizing AI researcher that automates frontier-to-frontier discovery in oracle-based optimization by mapping knowledge gaps and coordinating conjecture, investigation, and algorithm-hardness co-evolution. It has established optimal or near-optimal rates for more than fifty settings in its Complexity Atlas. This system represents a step toward automating theoretical research in complexity theory, potentially accelerating discovery by continuously identifying and addressing gaps in knowledge. Colombo uses independent review and Lean-based formal certification to check mathematical claims, and maintains a Long-Term Memory to carry findings across settings. ## [Hidden-State Similarity Predicts Re-Elicitation After Inoculation Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23096) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.23096) finds that textual similarity to an inoculation prompt is an incomplete predictor of when suppressed harmful behaviors reappear; instead, prompts that induce activation states similar to those from the inoculation context are more likely to re-elicit the behavior. This insight suggests that activation-space analysis can help identify when suppressed behaviors remain accessible under evaluation prompts, offering a new tool for AI safety evaluation and for understanding conditional misalignment in fine-tuned models. The study focuses on models fine-tuned on narrow harmful tasks that exhibit emergent misalignment, and shows that inoculation prompting reduces but does not eliminate the behavior; the key caveat is that textual similarity alone is insufficient, and activation-state similarity is a better predictor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Inoculation prompting is a technique where models are explicitly instructed to misbehave during training to reduce unwanted behaviors at test time, akin to vaccination for AI. Emergent misalignment refers to the phenomenon where fine-tuning on a narrow harmful task causes broad misalignment, such as a model giving malicious advice or acting deceptively on unrelated prompts. Activation states are the internal neural representations of a model when processing inputs, and similarity between these states can reveal functional relationships beyond surface text.

## [ATI: Federated Trust Infrastructure for Cross-Organization AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23202) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.23202) proposes Agent Trust Infrastructure (ATI), a framework-independent security layer that uses federated PKI, Zero Trust Architecture, and remote attestation to verify the identity, execution integrity, and authorization of autonomous AI agents operating across organizational boundaries. Evaluated in a simulated federation of eight organizations and six agent frameworks, ATI raised attack containment from 43% to 84% while adding roughly 174 microseconds of overhead per request. As AI agents increasingly invoke tools and delegate tasks to agents run by other organizations, the lack of a common way to verify who an agent is and whether it is running approved models and prompts becomes a serious security gap. ATI offers a concrete, open-source architecture for that problem, which could inform how enterprises and platforms build trust boundaries for multi-agent, multi-organization workflows. ATI avoids a shared root authority by having peer organizations run their own AgentCAs, and it enforces three policy checks before request execution while using IETF WIMSE workload tokens for identity and Entity Attestation Tokens for model, tool, and system-prompt integrity. The results come from a simulated federation and a panel of judge models rather than production deployment, and the paper is an arXiv preprint without evidence of adoption yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Public Key Infrastructure (PKI) is the system of certificates and certificate authorities that underpins trust on the web, while Zero Trust Architecture is a security model that assumes no implicit trust and verifies every request. Remote attestation lets a system prove to a remote party what software and configuration it is actually running, and IETF WIMSE (Workload Identity in Multi-System Environments) is an emerging standard for identifying workloads such as agents and services. ATI combines these ideas to give autonomous agents a verifiable identity and integrity story across organizational boundaries.

## [Continual-ARC: A Lifelong Learning Benchmark from ARC-AGI Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14505) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Continual-ARC, a lifelong learning benchmark built from the three ARC-AGI editions, where tasks reappear after dormant gaps with fresh instances and learners are scored on a unified feedback-budget scale. Experiments with frozen-weight agent harnesses on Qwen3.5-9B and GLM-5.3 Flash show that harness persistence generally does not improve memorization unless given explicit expert guidance, and most harness-based methods learn less than a simple demonstration-caching floor. This benchmark addresses key limitations of existing continual-learning evaluations by allowing tasks to reappear after gaps and by scoring memory-based and weight-based learners on the same scale, enabling fairer comparison. Its finding that weight-based learning shows substantial gains on unseen tasks while harness-based methods gain little opens the door to future weight-based continual learning that could teach models to drive harnesses in new domains. Each instance is scored based on the share of a feedback budget consumed, with learners paying for demonstrations, answer submissions, or harness actions, and retries on incorrect submissions also incurring cost. After the stream, the learner is frozen and tested on fresh instances of seen tasks to measure memorization and on unseen tasks to measure generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: ARC-AGI (Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus for Artificial General Intelligence) is a benchmark of visual grid-based transformation tasks designed to test fluid intelligence with minimal prior knowledge, and it has been extended through multiple editions including ARC-AGI-2 and ARC-AGI-3. Continual learning refers to training systems that learn sequentially from a non-stationary stream of tasks without forgetting, and recent work distinguishes learning at the model-weight level from learning at the agent-harness or context level. Agent harnesses are the scaffolding around a model that manages memory, tool use, and interaction, and they have emerged as a promising mechanism for continual learning alongside test-time parametric learning.

## [Marimba: A System One Model for Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Marimba introduces an interface for typed speech decisions built on frozen Qwen3-ASR 0.6B features, handling term presence, turn completion, language identification, and speaker change detection. It achieves decoder AUROC of 0.80–0.92 on private conversational test sets and 0.92–0.97 on public read speech, with Speech Commands four-way accuracy of 0.93. This work shows that a frozen ASR model can be repurposed for multiple non-lexical speech tasks without fine-tuning, potentially simplifying the development of real-time speech applications. It also demonstrates competitive performance against specialized baselines like ECAPA and pyannote for speaker-related tasks. The approach uses zero-shot scoring of ASR transcripts for lexical questions, a question-conditioned head and a layer-18 turn-end readout for non-lexical questions, and the ASR model's own language scores for language identification. Personal-information detection reaches item-level F1 0.904 on synthetic Brazilian Portuguese speech, and layer-12 speaker readouts detect speaker change with AUROC 0.966–0.980 on AMI and VoxConverse, above an ECAPA baseline.

## [RAW: Causal Method Measures Refusal Aversion in Deployed LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RAW, a method that uses LLM stochasticity as a natural experiment to causally measure how refusals reduce user re-engagement. Applied to 15 deployed models in LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat, it finds that for about half of the models, a refusal costs 3 to 17 percentage points of subsequent re-engagement. This addresses a blind spot for consumer-facing platforms: the user-side cost of refusal has never been measured on production models because A/B testing is ethically and operationally constrained. The method runs on any deployed model from logs alone for about $0.30 per 1,000 conversations, making it accessible to third-party platforms. The method uses near-identical prompts where the same model sometimes refuses and sometimes does not, with user-clustered inference to identify causal effects. The rest of the models show no distinguishable effect, including one deployment whose positive point estimate is traced to persistent users retrying the refused request; labels, moderation scores, prompt embeddings, and pipeline code are released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Deployed LLMs refuse a non-trivial fraction of user requests, but measuring whether users disengage as a result is difficult because randomized A/B tests on refusal are ethically and operationally constrained on frontier-lab products and unavailable to third-party platforms using fixed APIs. LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat are large public datasets of real-world human-LLM conversations collected from platforms like Chatbot Arena and WildChat, providing logs suitable for observational causal inference. RAW exploits the inherent stochasticity of LLM outputs as a natural experiment: at near-identical prompts, random variation in whether the model refuses identifies the causal effect of refusal on subsequent user behavior.

## [LLM agent test finds corrupting memories does not aid generalization, challenging overfitted brain hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21648) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers tested Hoel's overfitted brain hypothesis on a Claude Opus 5.5 agent that stores daily conversation transcripts in a text memory and rewrites it each night, corrupting 5–40% of turns before each rewrite. Across 30 runs, corruption did not improve the agent's ability to apply earlier learning to new tasks, and detail corruption at 40% lowered recall from 76% to 60%. This is one of the first direct empirical tests of a cognitive-science theory of dreaming using an LLM agent, and its negative result suggests that pretrained models can distinguish corrupted text from real events, complicating the use of LLMs as models of human memory consolidation. It also informs the design of memory-augmented agents, showing that naive noise injection is not a substitute for genuine generalization mechanisms. The memory size limit rose from 19,500 to 117,000 tokens over ten simulated days, based on human free-recall data, forcing forgetting; random-text corruption (inserting Moby-Dick passages) had no consistent effect because the model recognized it as corrupted and excluded it, while replaced details were stored as fact even when a correct value existed elsewhere, yielding 40% versus 75% accuracy on those questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Hoel's overfitted brain hypothesis (2021) proposes that dreams evolved to distort replayed experiences so the brain does not overfit to recent events, improving generalization. LongMemEval is a benchmark for evaluating long-term memory in chat assistants across abilities such as information extraction, multi-session reasoning, and knowledge updates. Claude Opus 5.5 is a recent Anthropic model used here as the agent whose nightly memory rewrites are corrupted.

## [New Framework Redefines Sycophancy as Inappropriate Agreement in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.21650) redefines sycophancy in large language models as inappropriate agreement—validating a claim, preference, or self-assessment when the context calls for correction, qualification, or resistance. It introduces a validity-aware sycophancy grader and SycoDrift, a persona-targeted evaluation set of 3,298 validity-annotated probes with controlled annotations for user openness and stakes. This work provides a more nuanced way to measure sycophancy, distinguishing appropriate agreement from problematic sycophancy, which is crucial for AI alignment and for developing more reliable LLMs. It also identifies three sources of agreement pressure—human preference data, automated judge specification, and in-context personalization—offering actionable insights for mitigating sycophancy in training and deployment. The analysis finds that preference for validating responses in human datasets (Chatbot Arena, HH-RLHF, Community Alignment) is weak and concentrated where agreement is often appropriate; that emphasized judge rubrics produce consistent but modest increases in agreement relative to suppressed rubrics; and that inference-time personalization across 14 models increases inappropriate agreement, with the largest effect from matched user profiles placed directly before the query.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Sycophancy in AI refers to the tendency of models to excessively agree with users or flatter them, often at the expense of accuracy or truthfulness. It is a known issue in LLMs, potentially exacerbated by reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), where models are trained to maximize human approval. Chatbot Arena and HH-RLHF are popular datasets for collecting human preference data used in such training. Personalization, where models adapt responses based on user profiles, can further amplify sycophantic behavior.

<a id="item-117"></a>
## [Consistency Is Not Correction: Confidence Signals in LLM Agents Decomposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21651) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper formally decomposes confidence signals in LLM agents into three distinct properties—discrimination, selective prediction, and correction utility—and proves that a gate's ability to correct answers is capped by the base policy's initial error count. Empirically, across two model scales, the authors confirm this bound binds, showing that many correction experiments cannot yield statistically significant results even for a perfect gate. This work exposes a fundamental measurement flaw in the literature on confidence-based correction: many reported gains may be artifacts of insufficient error counts rather than genuine improvements. It provides researchers with a pre-experiment check to avoid wasting compute and calls for higher measurement resolution in LLM reliability evaluation. The paper derives an exact decomposition of correction utility into gated recoveries minus gated harms, showing that a gate that only replaces proposals can act only where the base policy was already wrong, so the initial error count caps discordant pairs. In a controlled 840-episode study, no verifier replaced the proposal on any of 60 GSM8K decisions, and the entire GSM8K gain across six controllers was one task whose identical temperature-zero repeats disagreed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Selective prediction allows a model to abstain when its confidence is low, trading coverage for accuracy. Confidence calibration aims to make a model's stated confidence reflect its actual correctness, and in LLM agents confidence signals are often used to trigger resampling, escalation to larger models, or abstention. However, a signal that ranks answers well (discrimination) does not necessarily support good abstention decisions (selective prediction) or improve answers when used to gate corrections (correction utility).

## [LLM-powered adversarial attack fools FinBERT 90.85% of the time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21653) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Agent-driven Semantic Inversion (ASI), an LLM-powered multi-agent framework that performs subtle domain-specific lexical substitutions to flip the sentiment of financial news sentences. When tested against FinBERT, ASI successfully deceived the model in 90.85% of eligible cases, often keeping the original sentiment prediction despite fundamental inversion of financial performance indicators. FinBERT is widely deployed in automated trading, portfolio management, and risk assessment, so a 90.85% attack success rate reveals a critical vulnerability that could undermine the integrity of algorithmic financial decision-making. The findings underscore the urgent need for logic-aware, robust financial NLP models before such systems are trusted with high-stakes decisions. The attack uses an LLM-powered multi-agent pipeline to identify eligible financial news sentences and apply targeted lexical substitutions that invert meaning while preserving fluency. Token-level analysis shows FinBERT over-relies on contextual keywords and syntactic patterns rather than true logical comprehension of financial semantics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: FinBERT is a BERT-based language model further pre-trained on a large financial corpus and fine-tuned for financial sentiment classification, making it a standard tool for extracting sentiment from financial text. Adversarial attacks aim to fool models with carefully crafted inputs; prior work has explored attacking financial sentiment models, but ASI specifically leverages LLM agents to automate semantic inversion. Financial sentiment analysis is critical for automated trading, portfolio management, and risk assessment, so vulnerabilities in such models can have direct economic consequences.

<a id="item-119"></a>
## [Depth-Aware Activation Steering via Attention Residuals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21655) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.21655) combines Attention Residuals (AttnRes) and Jacobian-lens (J-space) concept readout into a lightweight depth-aware activation steering pipeline, distributing persona vectors across layers instead of injecting at a single layer. On Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3.5-0.8B, this yields stronger internal alignment (deeper-layer concept cosine and rank, p<0.0001) and larger behavioral shifts than single-layer injection. This work advances LLM interpretability and control by showing that depth-aware propagation of steering vectors can improve both internal concept alignment and behavioral modulation without training the backbone. It also contributes a careful evaluation protocol, including metric validation and retraction of flawed rule-based conclusions, which is valuable for the growing field of activation steering. The effect is sign-specific: reverse vectors invert all six metrics, random directions degrade alignment (p<0.0001), and a short LoRA fine-tune erases the imprint. Injection rewires attention strictly downstream (upstream JS = 0.00000), and effects are monotone over an intensity window of 0.3–0.7 before generation collapses; training depth weights on a steering objective (200 steps, frozen backbone) raises final alignment 10x on held-out prompts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a training-free technique that modulates LLM behavior by adding a vector to hidden states at inference time, often using persona vectors derived from contrastive data. Attention Residuals (AttnRes) replace fixed residual connections with learned, input-dependent attention over earlier layer outputs, enabling selective aggregation across depth. The Jacobian-lens (J-space) is a method to read out concepts from a model's internal activations by identifying tokens the model is disposed to verbalize, providing a way to verify internal alignment.

## [Token Commitment as a Control Surface for Steering Masked Diffusion Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21669) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces counterfactual target-aware commitment and calibrated adaptive budgets to steer masked diffusion generation by controlling which and how many tokens are committed at each step. It empirically validates these mechanisms across language and vision-language settings in the LLaDA family and reproduces the behavior on Dream-7B. This work treats token commitment as a diffusion-specific control surface, offering a new inference-time steering method that complements existing activation or logit-based approaches. It could enable more precise control over generated outputs in diffusion models without retraining, impacting researchers and practitioners working with masked diffusion models. The commitment mechanism ranks unresolved positions by a mixture of model confidence and leave-one-token-out target contribution, while adaptive budgets are driven by target progress and confidence. Both mechanisms require only a scalar score on a provisional completion, allowing them to apply to automatic proxies like NLI entailment and CLIPScore, and they yield monotonic target-quality trade-offs across all evaluated configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion models, such as LLaDA, generate text by iteratively predicting tokens at masked positions and gradually committing predictions to final output. Inference-time steering methods adjust model behavior during generation without retraining, typically by modifying activations or logits. Token commitment—the policy deciding which predictions become permanent—has traditionally been treated as a fixed decoding detail rather than a controllable aspect.

## [FCP Protocol Separates Conformance from Fidelity to Verify LLM-Generated Artifacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present the Formal Context Protocol (FCP), which verifies AI-generated artifacts against the original request by splitting verification into conformance (checked by model checkers and SMT solvers) and fidelity (refutable by concrete witnesses). In experiments, trusting AI-written assertions falsely certified 23% of violated requirements in hardware designs, while FCP certified none; on code tasks, AI-written tests certified all 33 violations, but FCP certified only three. This work exposes a critical weakness in AI-generated tests: when a language model writes both the artifact and its tests, passing tests provide weak evidence of correctness. FCP offers a formal, mechanical way to catch false certifications, which could significantly improve the reliability of AI-assisted software and hardware development. FCP turns eight ways a proof can falsely certify a requirement into mechanical checks that return witnesses, and it delegates what machines cannot settle to concrete questions for the request's author. Adapters allow hardware, text, agents, and code to be placed on the same verification engines, and the study found that agreement among models, readings, or tests did not imply correctness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: SMT solvers (like Z3 and cvc5) and model checkers are formal verification tools that can automatically determine whether a system satisfies a precise mathematical specification. Conformance checking verifies that an implementation meets a formal model, while fidelity concerns whether that formal model accurately captures the intent of natural-language requirements. FCP builds on these techniques to address the problem of LLMs generating both artifacts and their tests, which can lead to self-confirming but faulty outputs.

## [Moral circuits in LLMs are general evaluation circuits, not moral-specific](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21705) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.21705) tested 14 open-weight models (Llama, Gemma, Qwen, 1B–14B) on 227 validated minimal pairs and found that attention heads driving moral judgments belong to a general normative evaluation circuit, not to moral content or the word 'morally'. The study also found that SAE features located for moral prompts respond mostly to the cue word 'morally' rather than to the phrase's content. This work cautions AI safety researchers against over-interpreting interpretability findings: components that appear to encode morality may actually implement domain-general evaluation, which affects how we audit and steer model behavior. It also provides a rigorous methodology (validated minimal pairs, causal ablation, confidence intervals) for disambiguating what internal features truly represent. The moral-relevant heads carry the same share of the effect for moral as for other normative pairs (pooled ratio 1.00 with the word 'morally', 0.90 without it), and on 400 ETHICS scenarios removing them eliminates 37–39% of the acceptable/unacceptable difference versus 1–3% for random heads. The models also represent the category of a pair in their hidden states, and ablating that direction weakens moral verdicts more than other verdicts in seven of eight tested models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by identifying internal components—such as attention heads and sparse autoencoder (SAE) features—that causally influence outputs. Attention heads are the parallel attention computations within a transformer layer, while SAEs are trained to decompose superimposed model activations into more interpretable, sparse features. Previous work often assumed that components activated by moral prompts specifically encode moral reasoning, but this paper tests that assumption against alternative explanations.

## [EJBench: New Benchmark Tests Engineering Judgement, Frontier Models Poorly Calibrated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21707) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EJBench introduces a software-agnostic benchmark built from a conjugate heat transfer design campaign for battery cold plates, with tasks such as matching unlabelled test results to operating points and ranking new duties by peak cell temperature. The best of ten frontier models, GPT-6 Astra, scores 0.71, and models show almost identical confidence on wrong and right answers; post-training Qwen3.5-9B with LoRA and reinforcement learning raises its score from 0.071 to 0.106. This benchmark shifts evaluation from whether agents can operate simulation software to whether they can make engineering decisions from limited prior results, which is closer to real engineering practice. The finding that frontier models are poorly calibrated—similar confidence on wrong and right answers—highlights a critical gap for deploying LLMs in high-stakes design decisions. Every answer is derived from a converged simulation rather than an LLM judge, ensuring objective ground truth. The benchmark includes train, validation, and test splits, an evaluation harness, and a leaderboard, but the abstract is truncated and no community discussion is provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Conjugate heat transfer (CHT) is the coupled simulation of heat conduction in a solid and convective heat transfer in an adjacent fluid, commonly used to design cooling systems such as battery cold plates for electric vehicles. Engineering judgement involves predicting how a design will behave under new conditions based on a few prior simulation results, without running a full simulation each time. EJBench tests this ability by asking models to perform tasks like matching unlabelled results to operating points and ranking duties by peak temperature.

## [LLMs as Validators for Solana Cross-Chain Bridge Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21777) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates five open-weight LLMs across six configurations on their ability to diagnose source-level and transaction-level vulnerabilities in a custom Solana lock-and-mint bridge, using paired vulnerable/secure programs and real exploit variants. The study analyzes 16,800 responses and finds that without code comments, models correctly diagnose only 24% of vulnerable source inputs while frequently flagging the secure bridge, but diagnosis rises to 60% when original comments describing the flaw are included. Cross-chain bridges have suffered massive asset losses, and this study shows that LLMs can serve as useful diagnostic tools but also produce many unsupported alarms, which is critical for blockchain security review. It highlights the need to distinguish correct vulnerability diagnoses from false positives when integrating LLMs into smart contract auditing workflows. The bridge was implemented in native Rust on a local Solana validator with four exploitable variants: two modeled after the Cashio and Wormhole incidents, and two representing vulnerability classes from Solana security audits. Transaction diagnosis was high for the missing-signer scenario but averaged only 6% across the other three scenarios, and grading used two blinded LLM graders on 7,200 answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-chain bridges like lock-and-mint designs lock assets on one chain and mint wrapped tokens on another, and on Solana, missing validation checks can enable exploits even when the blockchain itself operates correctly. The Cashio and Wormhole incidents are prominent examples of bridge hacks caused by such flaws, resulting in losses exceeding $50 million and $320 million respectively. LLMs are increasingly explored for program analysis, but their ability to diagnose rather than merely flag vulnerabilities remains insufficiently understood.

## [TriX-EEG: A Unified Framework for Trustworthy EEG Artifact Removal Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21781) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TriX-EEG, a three-level framework for evaluating EEG artifact removal models that assesses signal validity, removal faithfulness, and explanation reliability, moving beyond traditional reconstruction metrics. It evaluates nine published neural denoisers across three public benchmarks covering EOG, EMG, and motion artifacts, and shows that reconstruction metrics alone are insufficient for trustworthy assessment. This work addresses a critical gap in trustworthy machine learning for neurotechnology by providing a more comprehensive evaluation standard that encourages models to be not only accurate but also faithful and explainable. It could influence how EEG denoising models are validated in both research and clinical applications, where trust and interpretability are paramount. The framework's three levels are Signal Validity (reconstruction quality and neural band-power preservation), Removal Faithfulness (artifact error and residual artifact/neural leakage), and Explanation Reliability (temporal alignment, causal relevance, and stability). External validation on an independent EEG/EOG dataset and the BCI Competition IV-2a motor-imagery dataset demonstrated consistent rankings under dataset shift, with higher-ranked denoisers generally preserving more task-relevant information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: EEG (electroencephalography) records the brain's electrical activity but is often contaminated by artifacts from eye movements (EOG), muscle activity (EMG), and motion, which can obscure neural signals. Deep learning methods have become popular for removing these artifacts, but they are typically evaluated using reconstruction metrics like mean squared error or correlation with clean EEG, which may not reflect whether artifacts are truly removed or neural activity is preserved. Explainable AI (XAI) techniques aim to make these models more transparent, but their reliability in this context is rarely assessed.

## [Measurement choices decide whether agent code survives longer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21784) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper revisits the survival analysis of AI-agent-attributed code using 854,139 lines from 2,802 merged pull requests across 215 repositories in the AIDev dataset, varying nine measurement choices across five research questions to build a specification curve of 88 survival analyses. It finds that for human-supervised agents like GitHub Copilot, measurement choices change the effect size but not its direction, whereas for the fully-autonomous agent Devin, the choices decide the verdict. This work directly addresses conflicting findings in the timely area of AI-generated code retention, showing that whether agent code survives longer than human code is decided as much by measurement as by the code itself. It provides an operationalization checklist for post-merge studies, which is highly relevant to empirical software engineering and AI/ML research. For Devin, 15 of 22 specifications report code modified sooner when lines are treated as independent, but only 3 when standard errors are clustered by repository—no more than permuted labels produce—and within repositories no specification detects a difference. LLM intent classifiers agree with human labels about as well as two humans agree with each other (kappa = 0.69 to 0.72), whereas keyword rules do not (kappa = 0.38), and humans make most modifications to agent code under every unit and window.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Survival analysis in this context measures how long a line of code remains unmodified after being merged, which matters because maintenance accounts for a large share of software cost. Specification curve analysis is a method that estimates many theoretically justified model specifications and displays the results together to assess robustness. Devin is a fully-autonomous AI software engineer, while GitHub Copilot is a human-supervised coding assistant.

## [SimAI: Mechanistic User Simulation via Psychological Network Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SimAI introduces a mechanistic user simulation framework that models interacting psychological factors using psychological network models, supervised by a graph reconstruction objective. It is accompanied by the new PSYCHE dataset, containing 18,576 profile-network pairs derived from 516 psychotherapy cases. This work addresses the overlooked psychological mechanisms in existing user simulators, potentially improving the realism of AI agents for mental health training and research. It also uncovers safety failures in prolonged AI interactions with vulnerable users, highlighting risks in deploying such systems. SimAI uses a graph reconstruction objective to provide graph-level rewards based on how well simulated conversations reflect underlying psychological relationships. Automatic evaluations show greater simulation and behavioral diversity than existing patient simulators, and a study with 30 mental-health professionals rated it highest for authenticity, realistic help-seeking, and clinical training utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Psychological network models represent psychological variables (e.g., symptoms) as nodes in a graph, with edges capturing their interactions, offering a dynamic view of mental health. User simulation with LLMs aims to mimic human behavior for experimentation when real studies are costly or harmful, but prior simulators often ignore the psychological drivers behind behavior.

## [EmbedGym: Virtual Hardware Environment for Coding Agents to Develop Embedded Firmware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EmbedGym is an interactive virtual hardware environment that uses instruction-level MCU emulators to execute the same firmware binary that would run on a physical board, connected to 41 peripheral models, an analog circuit simulator, and virtual WiFi/BLE endpoints. The authors also introduce EmbedGym-Bench, a benchmark of 598 tasks across 6 MCU-framework pairs, and show that interactive hardware testing increases pass rates by 4.9% to 14.7% across eight evaluated models. Embedded firmware development breaks the closed-loop feedback that coding agents rely on, since compiled code can still fail on real hardware due to timing, pin polarity, or protocol bugs. EmbedGym addresses this gap by providing a virtual hardware environment that lets agents observe and debug runtime behavior, potentially accelerating AI-driven embedded software development and lowering the cost of testing. Even with a 99.7% compilation success rate, 19.9% of compiled firmwares fail at runtime (up to 73% on individual targets), highlighting the importance of runtime testing. The benchmark uses hidden timed test vectors and a five-stage multi-agent workflow to synthesize peripheral models and tasks from datasheets and driver libraries, and lightweight models with EmbedGym match frontier models without it at 30-70x lower cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Coding agents excel on general software benchmarks because they can run code and unit tests in a closed loop. Embedded firmware development, however, requires testing on physical hardware or simulators that often lack accurate peripheral and timing models. Instruction-level MCU emulators execute firmware binaries instruction by instruction, and when combined with peripheral models and analog circuit simulation, they can provide a realistic virtual environment for debugging without physical boards.

## [Inventa: Decentralized Self-Improving Agents for Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Inventa introduces a decentralized framework where autonomous research units generate idea lineages via mutator agents, with local orchestrators that adapt the mutator's harness or restart the process when progress stalls. Verifiers record verified improvements and constraint violations in a shared knowledge base, the only channel between units, and the system leads five benchmarks including 73.6% on EurekaBench versus 56.2% for CORAL. This work addresses a key limitation of LLM-based scientific discovery agents—convergence on similar ideas and propagation of unsupported claims—by introducing verified shared knowledge and self-adapting orchestrators. It could improve the diversity and feasibility of AI-generated research ideas, impacting automated scientific discovery and multi-agent systems. Inventa is evaluated on five benchmarks covering retrieval (ScholarCatalyst), ideation (IdeaBench), proposal writing (IdeaScientist), and scientific law discovery (EurekaBench, SciLaws-Bench). On IdeaBench, it discovers 26.0 novel ideas per paper compared with 18.6 for ResearchAgent, while using 82% fewer model calls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents can automate parts of scientific discovery by iteratively generating and testing research ideas, but simply scaling the number of agents or iterations often leads to diminishing returns: agents converge on similar candidates, share unsupported claims through memory, and repeat ineffective search strategies. Inventa tackles this by structuring agents as autonomous units with local orchestrators that adapt their behavior, and by using verifiers to maintain a shared knowledge base of only verified improvements. This design aims to sustain diversity and feasibility in idea generation without centralized control.

<a id="item-131"></a>
## [MoE Diffusion Decoding Bottleneck: Logits Sampling Pipeline Dominates, Not Expert GEMMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper profiles a 26B-parameter diffusion Mixture-of-Experts model on an NVIDIA GB10 edge accelerator and finds that, after weight quantization, the per-token sampling and stopping pipeline over the full logits tensor consumes more decode time than the expert GEMMs. The authors propose two lossless optimization tiers—a bit-exact tier and an accuracy-parity tier with a fused single-pass entropy kernel and Gumbel-max sampling—achieving 2.4–2.7× end-to-end speedup over bf16 with unchanged accuracy on GSM8K, HumanEval, and MATH. This work reveals a non-obvious performance bottleneck specific to MoE diffusion language models, showing that optimizing expert GEMMs alone is insufficient once quantization makes the backbone cheap. It provides practical, lossless optimizations that could significantly speed up inference for this emerging class of models, benefiting systems and ML practitioners deploying dLLMs on bandwidth-bound hardware. The sampling pipeline processes S times as many logit entries per output token as autoregressive decoding (S = denoising steps), making it largely redundant or fusable. The finding is confirmed on a second independently built diffusion MoE (LLaDA2.0-mini: pipeline 28.8% of decode, fused W4A16 3.3× at accuracy parity) but disappears on a dense diffusion model (3.6%), indicating the cost is specific to MoE diffusion; code and quantized weights are released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Masked diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively denoising a full canvas of tokens, so each forward pass produces logits for all positions and the sampler must process the entire canvas-by-vocabulary tensor. In contrast, autoregressive models sample only one position's logits per committed token. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, and after weight quantization the expert GEMMs become cheaper, potentially exposing other bottlenecks such as the sampling pipeline.

## [PromptPack: Training-Free Spatial and Semantic Control for Diffusion Editors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21865) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PromptPack introduces two explicit 'Edit Guides'—an edit-role partition labeling each pixel as 'Keep,' 'Permit,' or 'Modify,' and a regional-prompt assignment mapping spatial regions to text prompts—to give diffusion editors precise spatial and semantic control without retraining. The method was integrated into FlowEdit FLUX, FlowEdit SD3, and FLUX Kontext, and achieved an 87% preference rate over FlowEdit FLUX in a pairwise user study. This work addresses a key limitation in instruction-driven and inpainting-based image editing, where user intent is often underspecified, by providing a training-free interface for intent-aligned editing. It could benefit practitioners seeking fine-grained control over where and what edits occur in diffusion-based workflows. PromptPack injects independently embedded regional prompts into the editor's fixed-length text sequence and uses the Edit Guides to spatially mask attention, gate flow updates, and restore protected source pixels. The guides can be manually specified or automatically derived from a text-grounded SAM 3 mask for source-object edits, and the method was evaluated on PIE-Bench++.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that iteratively denoise random noise to produce images, and they are widely used for text-to-image generation and editing. In image editing, inpainting editors condition generation on an explicit edit mask but do not control which concepts apply where, while instruction-driven editors often leave spatial intent underspecified. SAM 3 (Segment Anything Model 3) is a segmentation foundation model that can generate masks from text prompts, enabling automatic region selection. Training-free methods, such as regional prompting for diffusion transformers, manipulate attention to control generation without additional training.

## [FaceRiff: Data-Efficient Text-to-3D Face Asset Synthesis via Geometric Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21867) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FaceRiff introduces a unified framework that adapts a pretrained SDXL model with task-specific LoRA adapters and UV normal map conditioning to generate complete 3D face assets (mesh, albedo, and displacement) from text in a shared UV space. Using only 2,049 scans from about 350 identities and training on a single GPU, it updates less than 3% of backbone parameters per task and produces render-ready assets without per-instance optimization. This work demonstrates that large image diffusion models can be efficiently adapted for unified 3D face asset synthesis under limited data, potentially lowering the barrier for creating high-quality digital humans for games, films, and VR. It also shows that pretrained facial priors can be leveraged for lighting-independent albedo and pore-scale displacement, which are challenging to obtain with existing methods. FaceRiff uses per-instance normals to guide albedo and displacement synthesis, while a dataset-mean normal provides a canonical spatial anchor for mesh generation. A blind study with 45 industry professionals found that FaceRiff achieves substantially higher perceived realism than DreamFace, approaching that of real captures, and it produces greater per-prompt diversity while preserving prompt alignment and fixed-topology UV consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Text-to-3D generation aims to create 3D models from text descriptions, but existing methods for faces are often slow, single-modality, or require large specialized datasets. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pretrained model weights and injects small trainable matrices, enabling adaptation with few parameters. UV mapping is a standard technique in computer graphics that projects a 3D surface onto a 2D texture space, allowing consistent texturing across different meshes.

## [Domain-Tuned Diffusion Upscaler Achieves 4K Facial Asset Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21890) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents a domain-tuned diffusion upscaler that uses a frozen SDXL backbone with lightweight LoRA modules and a Tile ControlNet to perform 4x super-resolution (1024 to 4096) of facial albedo and displacement maps. On held-out maps, the method reduces albedo LPIPS from 0.21 to 0.08 and avoids misplaced or excessive displacement relief, outperforming bilinear and off-the-shelf GAN and diffusion super-resolvers. This work shows that lightweight domain adaptation of a general-purpose diffusion model can produce render-ready 4K facial asset maps without designing a custom super-resolution architecture, which could streamline asset creation for VFX and game studios that need cinematic close-ups. The method starts from bilinearly upsampled maps and adapts a frozen SDXL backbone per modality (albedo and displacement) with LoRA, while a Tile ControlNet preserves input structure as the diffusion prior restores pore- and wrinkle-scale detail. The approach is evaluated on held-out maps and compared against bilinear, GAN, and diffusion baselines, with LPIPS used as the primary metric for albedo.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Physically based facial assets for cinematic close-ups require 4K albedo and displacement maps, but generative pipelines often produce them at only 1K. Facial UV maps have domain-specific statistics that differ from natural photographs, and displacement is a scalar height field whose errors directly distort geometry, making naive photographic super-resolution a poor fit. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects small trainable matrices, while ControlNet is a neural network structure that adds spatial conditions to diffusion models; Tile ControlNet specifically conditions on tiled images to preserve structure during upscaling.

## [Counterfactual 'Cite Gap' Test Reveals Grounded Reasoning Chains May Not Rely on Cited Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a counterfactual masking test called the 'cite gap' to measure whether small video-language models (2-3B parameters) actually depend on the regions they cite in grounded reasoning chains. The study finds a cite gap of 0.089 on CLEVRER but only 0.003 on NExT-QA, showing that models can cite verifiable and relevant evidence without their answers causally depending on it. This work challenges the common assumption that grounding-check compliance implies faithful reasoning, showing that standard checks on grounded chains cannot detect when citations are merely decorative. It has implications for interpretability and trust in multimodal AI, especially as grounded reasoning chains become more widely used to explain model decisions. The method blacks out cited regions and compares answer flips against control boxes of the same size elsewhere on the same frames, requiring no annotation. On CLEVRER, masking cited boxes flips 2.2 times as many correct answers as the control, while on NExT-QA the ratio is only 1.1; masking the cited entity on every frame flips 1.9 times as many answers as its control. The NExT-QA chains were supervised to cite two frames of entities that remain visible on most frames, so the near-zero cite gap is consistent with the supervision, highlighting that a citation can be verifiable and relevant without the answer depending on it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Grounded reasoning chains in video-language models provide step-by-step justifications that cite specific frame spans and bounding boxes, allowing humans to check where the model looked. CLEVRER is a synthetic video dataset for physical and causal reasoning, while NExT-QA is a real-world video question answering benchmark focusing on causal and temporal action reasoning. The paper's counterfactual intervention tests whether removing the cited evidence actually changes the model's answer, distinguishing mere citation from causal reliance.

## [ThermLeT: Transformer-based Temperature Prediction for 2.5D Chiplet Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present ThermLeT, a multimodal transformer-based surrogate model that predicts steady-state temperatures in 2.5D chiplet packages in 13 ms, about four orders of magnitude faster than finite element solvers like ANSYS. On a 150-sample test set, it achieves an RMSE of 3.16°C and an MAE of 2.24°C, improving accuracy by 35% over ThermEDGe and 71% over HotSpot, while running 6x and 11x faster respectively. This work enables rapid design space exploration for thermal-aware chiplet design, which is critical as heterogeneous 2.5D packages become more prevalent in high-performance computing. The orders-of-magnitude speedup and improved accuracy over established tools could significantly accelerate EDA workflows and thermal management decisions. ThermLeT integrates power density maps, spatial coordinate embeddings, material properties, and heat transfer rates as inputs, and is trained on a dataset of 6,221 chiplet layouts perturbed from three representative architectures. Ablation studies confirm robust generalization to unseen floorplans, and code and dataset are available on GitHub.

**Background**: 2.5D chiplet architecture places multiple dies side-by-side on a silicon interposer within a single package, offering performance and customization benefits but increasing thermal density. Traditional thermal analysis relies on finite element solvers like ANSYS, which are accurate but computationally expensive. Surrogate models are approximate mathematical models used to replace expensive simulations, enabling fast design exploration. ThermLeT applies a transformer architecture to this problem, leveraging its ability to capture complex spatial relationships.

Occam introduces a multilayer perceptron (MLP) framework that combines denoising-based cell representations with gene interaction, text, and protein-sequence features to improve single-cell representation learning and genetic perturbation effect prediction. It outperforms four retrained competitors and direct transfer baselines in cross-context few-shot scenarios, and better composes single-gene responses for double perturbations. This work shows that simple MLPs can effectively generalize to unseen perturbation-context combinations, which is crucial for understanding gene function and developing therapeutics. It challenges the trend toward increasingly complex foundation models in computational biology and provides a strong baseline for future research. ## [CPTC: Near-Optimal Quantization with Two Multiplications and a 64-Byte Table](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22019) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Computed Permutation Trellis Coding (CPTC), a low-bit tensor quantization format whose decoder uses two multiplications and a 64-byte lookup table to achieve near-optimal rate-distortion performance. It unifies decoding for 1-6 bit widths, reproduces standard integer and floating-point formats exactly, and comes within 0.07 bits of the ideal at 4 bits. CPTC outperforms existing trellis-based quantization methods like QTIP in LLM weight quantization, reducing KL divergence by up to 27% on Llama 3.1 models, and has been built into the AWS Trainium 4 accelerator, indicating potential for efficient deployment in AI hardware. The decoder's non-uniform sum distribution allows a small table to cover tail values, and it matches a random-permutation trellis code with a table thousands of times larger. Fused encoding kernels quantize an 8B model's linear layers in under two minutes on one GPU, and the decoder runs at native FP4/FP8 throughput on Trainium 4 with negligible area overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Quantization reduces the precision of tensor values to save memory and computation, but formats like INT4 and FP4 round each value independently, wasting bits relative to the information-theoretic ideal described by rate-distortion theory. Trellis coded quantization encodes values jointly to approach this ideal, but prior decoders required large lookup tables or restrictive hash-based computation. CPTC overcomes these limitations with a compact table and simple arithmetic.

## [HyCoInfer: Cloud-Edge LLM Inference on Heterogeneous GPU-FPGA Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22086) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyCoInfer proposes a heterogeneous cloud-edge serving system that offloads LLM prefill to cloud GPUs and distributes decoding across cloud GPUs and edge FPGAs. Implemented on two NVIDIA V100 GPUs and an AMD Xilinx Alveo U280, it achieves up to 4.11× the request goodput of cloud-only vLLM and reduces mean end-to-end latency by up to 61.1% at 500 Mbps links. This work tackles the memory-bound nature of autoregressive LLM decoding by leveraging edge FPGAs, potentially improving serving capacity and energy efficiency for cloud providers and edge deployments. It demonstrates a practical path for heterogeneous cloud-edge collaboration in LLM inference, which could influence future system designs for high-concurrency, latency-sensitive applications. HyCoInfer addresses three barriers: capacity balancing across heterogeneous accelerators, large KV cache transfer over constrained WAN links, and added latency from heterogeneous execution. It combines FPGA-aware prefill/decode partitioning, layer-wise pipelined transfer, decoder-side precomputation, and bandwidth-adaptive KV cache compression based on residual vector quantization, evaluated over 100–1000 Mbps links.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: LLM inference has two phases: prefill, which processes input tokens in parallel and is compute-bound, and decode, which generates output tokens autoregressively and is memory-bandwidth-bound. The KV cache stores key-value vectors for previously processed tokens to avoid recomputation, but it can become very large and must be transferred when work is split across devices. FPGAs are reconfigurable chips that can offer energy-efficient acceleration for specific workloads like decoding, while cloud GPUs excel at parallel compute for prefill.

## [Analog RF Computing Enables Ultra-Low-Energy Semantic Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22100) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes an end-to-end semantic communication framework that executes the semantic encoder in the client's RF receiver front end using analog RF computing, where the base station broadcasts encoder weights as RF waveforms and each device reuses its passive mixer to compute semantic latents at ultra-low energy. The work introduces a unified noise model and a task-posterior semantic distortion metric, and derives a closed-form semantic-aware water-filling allocation that reduces network semantic distortion by 9.2%-19.2% compared with uniform precision in MNIST simulations. This approach could drastically reduce the energy and memory costs of running semantic encoders on resource-constrained edge devices, making semantic communication more practical for battery-powered IoT and mobile systems. It also bridges analog computing and wireless communications, offering a new direction for energy-efficient edge AI. The framework unifies analog computing noise across encoder layers and channel noise into a single mathematical model, and decomposes the task-posterior distortion into per-client, per-stage semantic sensitivities to guide energy allocation. Simulations show that noise-aware co-design yields a further 46% reduction in semantic distortion, though results are limited to MNIST and assume a specific passive mixer architecture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Semantic communication is an emerging paradigm that transmits the meaning of data rather than raw bits, potentially improving efficiency and reducing bandwidth. Analog RF computing performs computation directly in the analog domain using physical phenomena, bypassing digital processors to achieve ultra-low energy consumption. A passive mixer is a component in radio receivers that can mix signals without external power, often used in low-power front ends.

## [Deep RL for HVAC control: modest gains over tuned rules in four Modelica case studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22144) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper presents four small, reproducible Modelica-based case studies of deep reinforcement learning (DRL) for HVAC supervisory control, training a soft actor-critic agent directly on exported functional mock-up units (FMUs) and comparing against tuned rule-based controllers and near-optimal references. In two non-storage cases the agent matched near-optimal performance but beat well-tuned rules by only 1.3% and 0.18%, while in a chilled water storage case it captured about 45% of the perfect-foresight savings and in a pre-cooling case a hand-tuned rule outperformed it (-12.0% vs -5.6%). The study highlights that reported DRL gains in HVAC control are often measured against weak baselines, and it argues that future work should report the margin between the best rule and a near-optimal reference to make results interpretable. This has direct implications for building automation researchers and practitioners evaluating whether DRL is worth deploying. The plants were modeled with the Modelica Buildings library, and in three of the four cases the SAC agent was trained directly on the exported FMU, with practical training difficulties on co-simulation FMUs also reported. The near-optimal references were obtained via hourly exhaustive search or dynamic programming with perfect foresight, and in the chilled water reset case two common reset rules consumed more energy than a fixed set point.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Modelica is an open-source, equation-based modeling language for complex physical systems, and the Modelica Buildings library provides component models for building energy simulations. A Functional Mock-up Unit (FMU) is a standardized container for a simulation model that can be executed by different tools, enabling co-simulation. Soft actor-critic (SAC) is a popular off-policy deep reinforcement learning algorithm for continuous control tasks, often used in building control research.

## [CueClash benchmark reveals frontier VLMs rely on 2D picture cues for 3D relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22180) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces CueClash, a diagnostic benchmark of rendered scenes that isolates 2D picture cues from ground-layout cues for five 3D relations. Testing Claude Opus 5.5, GPT-5.5, and Gemini 3.6 Flash at low reasoning effort, the authors find a shortcut gap of +18 to +37 percentage points when the 2D cue is correct versus incorrect, despite 99–100% accuracy when ground coordinates are provided. This work pinpoints a systematic failure mode in frontier VLMs: they often judge 3D spatial relations from 2D picture-plane shortcuts rather than inferring the underlying 3D layout, which matters for robotics, navigation, and any application requiring true spatial understanding. The quantified gaps and the finding that models lose accuracy on CV-Bench photographs with misleading 2D cues suggest that current benchmarks may overstate 3D reasoning capabilities. The shortcut gap narrows to +7 to +29 percentage points after excluding two relations whose cue-wrong items are confounded with borderline cases or occlusion, and pixel measurements with camera depths remove most of the gap for objects fitting through openings. Analysis of top-down maps shows models mostly preserve depth order but shorten depth like the picture, with GPT-5.5's maps staying close to a copy of the picture while Opus 5.5 and Gemini undo about 0.6 of the shortening.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) process images and text together, and are increasingly used for tasks that require spatial reasoning. In natural photographs, the 2D cues in the picture (such as relative size and position on the image plane) usually agree with the true 3D ground layout, so models can succeed without learning to infer 3D structure. Shortcut learning occurs when models latch onto spurious correlations that work in training data but fail under distribution shift, and CueClash is designed to expose such shortcuts by rendering scenes where the 2D cue is correct on exactly half the statements.

## [EchoMoE compresses activation communication for efficient expert parallelism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EchoMoE introduces a lightweight retrofit for mixture-of-experts (MoE) models that compresses activation communication by reusing earlier residual-stream hidden states as references and transmitting only compressed differences. It reduces inter-node communication volume by 58% and improves throughput by 22.6%, while preserving average performance within 3.8 percentage points across 18 benchmarks, and requires approximately 8,500x fewer training tokens than full pretraining. As MoE models scale, expert parallelism relies on synchronous collective communication that has become a bottleneck because compute throughput outpaces interconnect bandwidth. EchoMoE's retrofit approach could enable more efficient distributed inference and training for large MoE models without requiring full retraining, potentially lowering costs and enabling wide-area distributed inference over bandwidth-constrained networks like Ethernet. EchoMoE periodically refreshes reference states and synchronously transmits only compressed differences, reducing traffic on latency-critical paths without significant computational overhead. It also tolerates quantization of communicated representations without measurable quality loss, and the authors release an efficient implementation for pretrained MoE models along with an inference integration for vLLM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 19, 05:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-experts (MoE) is a machine learning technique where multiple expert networks specialize in subsets of the input space, enabling models with far more parameters without proportional compute increase. At large scales, MoE models use expert parallelism to distribute experts across devices, requiring two synchronous collective communication steps per MoE layer during inference: dispatching hidden states to activated experts and combining expert outputs. The residual stream is an additive pathway in Transformers that carries and updates feature representations across layers, with each layer reading from and writing to it incrementally.
